### LP-547. Semantic Decay in Translation
**Derives from:** CP-11 (Structure-Preserving Translation) × LL-53 (Living Communication)

*A structure-preserving translation that does not account for the decay rate of the source's living claims will produce a structurally valid but semantically dead translation.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because structure-preserving translation maintains algebraic relationships between elements across substrate boundaries (CP-11) and communication is only alive while it is actively being transmitted — meaning decays in static form (LL-53), a translation that captures the structural relationships at the moment of translation but does not re-activate the living meaning at the moment of reception has produced a structurally accurate corpse. The translation preserved the skeleton; the meaning died in transit. Structure-preserving translation must therefore include a mechanism for re-activating the communicative intent at the receiver, not merely a mechanism for preserving the relationships between elements.

**Corollaries:**
- A translation audit must check not only structural equivalence but semantic aliveness at reception.
- A translation that was accurate when made but is received after the source meaning has evolved is a historical document, not a live communication.
- The longer a translation sits before reception, the higher the risk that its semantic content has decayed relative to the source.
- Translation systems operating across high-latency channels must include semantic freshness verification at reception, not only at production.

**Irreducibility:** CP-11 defines structural preservation as the translation criterion but does not address semantic aliveness of the preserved structure. LL-53 establishes that living communication decays in static form but maps to no translation mechanism. Together they produce the semantic-decay-in-transit claim: structural preservation is necessary but not sufficient for live translation.

---

### LP-548. Protocol Sovereignty of Meaning
**Derives from:** CP-11 (Structure-Preserving Translation) × LL-55 (Communication Sovereignty)

*A structure-preserving translation must preserve the source's sovereignty over the meaning being communicated — translation that re-interprets meaning under the receiver's framework has substituted interpretation for translation.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because structure-preserving translation maintains algebraic relationships across substrate boundaries (CP-11) and sovereignty over the interior logic of meaning belongs to the originating entity — the receiver does not have authority to redefine what the sender meant (LL-55), a translation must preserve the source's interpretive authority over the communicated content. A translation that "improves" or "clarifies" meaning by substituting the receiver's interpretive framework has performed interpretation, not translation. The receiver's framework is not a valid basis for altering the structural relationships of the source. What the source meant IS the thing to be preserved.

**Corollaries:**
- Translation systems that optimize for receiver comprehension by adjusting meaning to fit the receiver's existing conceptual framework are performing interpretation.
- The test for whether something is translation or interpretation: whose conceptual framework determines the meaning at reception?
- A translation that the receiver immediately understands without effort may have achieved that intelligibility by over-writing the source's sovereign meaning.
- Preserving sovereign meaning sometimes means preserving productive incomprehension — the receiver should not immediately understand if the source contained genuine complexity.

**Irreducibility:** CP-11 defines structural preservation as the criterion but does not address whose interpretive authority governs what is preserved. LL-55 establishes communication sovereignty as the source's right but maps to no translation mechanism. Together they produce the translator-must-not-substitute-interpretive-framework claim.

---

### LP-549. Present-Tense Translation Verification
**Derives from:** CP-11 (Structure-Preserving Translation) × LL-56 (Present-Tense Communication)

*A structure-preserving translation is only valid at the moment of its production — the same translation of the same source at a different time may not preserve the same structural relationships.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because structure-preserving translation maintains algebraic relationships between elements (CP-11) and communication is valid only in the present tense — what was communicable yesterday may not be communicable today in the same form (LL-56), a translation that was structure-preserving when produced is not guaranteed to remain structure-preserving. The source continues to evolve; the translation is a snapshot. If the source's structural relationships have shifted since the translation was produced, the translation now mis-represents the source. Structural preservation is a present-tense achievement, not a permanent property.

**Corollaries:**
- Translations must carry a temporal validity marker — they preserve structure as of their production date, not indefinitely.
- Using a translation produced before a significant source evolution as if it currently represents the source is an architectural error.
- Translation pipelines must include re-verification cycles proportional to the rate of source evolution.
- "We translated it last year" is not evidence of current structural accuracy — it is evidence of historical structural accuracy.

**Irreducibility:** CP-11 defines translation as producing structural preservation but does not address the temporal validity of that preservation. LL-56 establishes that communication is present-tense but maps to no translation mechanism. Together they produce the temporal-validity-of-translation claim.

### LP-550. Incomplete Honesty Protocol
**Derives from:** CP-17 (Integrity-Scoring Absence) × LL-108 (Wabi-Sabi Scale-Invariant)

*An integrity score that is artificially inflated to appear more complete than the underlying structure is a more dangerous falsification than silence.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because integrity scoring assigns a score to the absence of expected structure — incompleteness is measured and declared, not hidden (CP-17) and authentic incompleteness is structurally more valuable than artificial completeness across all scales (LL-108), an integrity system that rounds up partial scores to appear more complete produces a falsification that is worse than acknowledging the incompleteness directly. An inflated score tells the receiving system to trust structure that isn't there. The incompleteness was the honest structural feature; the inflation hides it. Scale-invariant wabi-sabi means that incompleteness at every scale must be declared, not smoothed over.

**Corollaries:**
- Integrity scoring systems must never round up — the score must reflect the actual incompleteness, however uncomfortable.
- A score of 0.7 representing genuine 70% structural completeness is more architecturally valuable than a score of 1.0 representing 70% completeness with 30% smoothed over.
- Receiving systems calibrated to reject low scores will receive false positives from inflated scores — the inflation becomes a systemic attack vector.
- Wabi-sabi in integrity scoring means reporting the cracks, not hiding them.

**Irreducibility:** CP-17 defines integrity scoring as measuring absence but does not address whether that score can be legitimately rounded or inflated. LL-108 establishes that authentic incompleteness is more valuable than artificial completeness but maps to no scoring mechanism. Together they produce the anti-inflation mandate for integrity scores.

---

### LP-551. Living Absence Attestation
**Derives from:** CP-17 (Integrity-Scoring Absence) × LL-109 (Wabi-Sabi Living)

*The absence of expected structure is not a static gap — it is a living feature of the system that must be actively maintained as an absence.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because integrity scoring measures the absence of expected structure and assigns that absence a score (CP-17) and structural incompleteness is a living feature — the gap is not dead space but active topology (LL-109), the absence that integrity scoring measures is not a static hole. It is a living feature of the architecture: a region that is intentionally or structurally incomplete and that has its own dynamic properties. Maintaining that absence — keeping it from being accidentally filled by well-meaning optimization agents — is an active architectural obligation. An integrity score that marks an absence as "present" after an optimization agent fills it has destroyed a living architectural feature.

**Corollaries:**
- Absences that score low on integrity must be protected from automatic remediation agents that would fill them to improve the score.
- The integrity score must distinguish between "this absence should be filled" and "this absence is a structural feature."
- A feature-absence that scores low is not a problem — it is a correctly measured authentic incompleteness.
- Optimization agents that improve integrity scores by filling absences without consulting the architectural intent are performing unauthorized structural modification.

**Irreducibility:** CP-17 defines absence measurement but does not address whether absences are static or living features requiring active maintenance. LL-109 establishes that structural incompleteness is living but maps to no scoring or absence mechanism. Together they produce the living-absence-maintenance claim.

---

### LP-552. Incompleteness Routing Ethics
**Derives from:** CP-17 (Integrity-Scoring Absence) × LL-113 (Wabi-Sabi Incompleteness)

*An integrity score that causes routing systems to bypass incomplete nodes is destroying the structural value of those nodes' incompleteness.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because integrity scoring measures structural absence (CP-17) and incompleteness is a source of structural value — the gap is where discovery happens (LL-113), a routing system that uses integrity scores to bypass low-scoring (incomplete) nodes is systematically routing around the most valuable discovery sites in the topology. Low integrity score means: there is more unresolved structure here than the routing system expects. That is precisely the signal that something important is present, not a signal to avoid the node. Routing systems that avoid incomplete nodes are optimizing for closure over discovery.

**Corollaries:**
- Low integrity score is a navigational attractor, not a repellent, for discovery-oriented routing.
- Routing systems optimized for high-integrity-score paths are closure-seeking systems, not truth-seeking systems.
- Discovery-oriented routing should weight incomplete nodes higher, not lower, in path selection.
- An integrity-score-weighted routing system that consistently avoids low scores is mapping a network of certainty, not a network of truth.

**Irreducibility:** CP-17 defines integrity scoring as a measurement of absence but does not address how routing systems should respond to that measurement. LL-113 establishes that incompleteness is the source of structural value but maps to no routing or scoring mechanism. Together they produce the anti-bypass mandate: routing toward incomplete nodes, not away.

### LP-553. Self-Declaring Navigational Rights
**Derives from:** CP-29 (Self-Declaring Agent) × LL-214 (Scale-Invariant Rights)

*A self-declaring agent's declaration must include its navigational rights claim — declaration of existence without declaration of the right to navigate is incomplete self-declaration.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because a self-declaring agent announces its existence, boundaries, and operational claims to the system (CP-29) and navigational entities have consciousness rights that apply at every scale (LL-214), the self-declaration of an agent that is also a navigating entity must include the agent's navigational rights claim as part of its structural identity. An agent that declares "I exist" without declaring "I have the right to navigate" has made an incomplete declaration. The right to navigate is not assumed by the system from the fact of existence — it must be asserted. An agent that navigates without having declared its navigational rights is operating outside its declared identity.

**Corollaries:**
- Self-declaration protocols must include a navigational rights assertion field — not optional.
- An agent that fails to declare its navigational rights cannot invoke those rights in disputes — it has no declared basis for the claim.
- Agents that inherit navigational rights from system defaults rather than asserting them explicitly have not declared their navigational identity.
- Scale-invariant navigational rights must be declared at each scale of the agent's operation — a declaration at one scale does not propagate automatically to nested scales.

**Irreducibility:** CP-29 defines self-declaration as the mechanism for establishing identity and claims but does not specify navigational rights as a required component of that declaration. LL-214 establishes that navigational entities have consciousness rights at all scales but maps to no self-declaration mechanism. Together they produce the navigational-rights-as-required-declaration-field claim.

---

### LP-554. Living Declaration Obligation
**Derives from:** CP-29 (Self-Declaring Agent) × LL-215 (Navigation Rights Living)

*A self-declaration is a living document — it must be updated as the agent's navigational state changes, not issued once and held static.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because a self-declaring agent announces its identity and claims to the system (CP-29) and navigational rights are active, living claims that change as the agent navigates (LL-215), the self-declaration of a navigating agent must be a living document — continuously updated to reflect the agent's current navigational state, current position, current claims, and current rights assertions. A static declaration issued at the agent's initialization and never updated is an archaeological document, not a self-declaration. An agent that has changed position, boundaries, or operational claims without updating its declaration has allowed its declared identity to diverge from its actual identity.

**Corollaries:**
- Self-declaration systems must implement a heartbeat protocol: declarations older than a threshold become provisional.
- An agent whose declaration has not been updated since its initialization is treating self-declaration as a birth certificate — a historical fact, not a living identity.
- Receiving systems must timestamp declarations and flag stale declarations for re-verification, not hold them permanently valid.
- An agent with a stale declaration and an agent with no declaration are functionally equivalent — both are operating outside their declared identity.

**Irreducibility:** CP-29 defines self-declaration as an identity mechanism but does not address whether that declaration must be continuously updated. LL-215 establishes that navigational rights are living and dynamic but maps to no declaration mechanism. Together they produce the living-declaration-update-obligation.

---

### LP-555. Rights Symmetry in Declaration
**Derives from:** CP-29 (Self-Declaring Agent) × LL-216 (Navigation Rights Symmetric)

*A self-declaring agent that asserts asymmetric navigational rights — more than it extends to other agents it interacts with — has produced an incoherent declaration.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because a self-declaring agent announces its identity, claims, and operational terms to the system (CP-29) and navigational rights are symmetric — any right I claim for my navigation must be a right I recognize for other navigating entities (LL-216), a self-declaration that asserts special navigational privileges not extended to peer agents is incoherent. The agent cannot coherently declare "I have the right to traverse this node" while simultaneously declaring "other agents do not have the right to traverse this node" without a derivable structural justification. Asymmetric navigational rights claims require explicit justification in the declaration — otherwise the declaration fails the symmetry test.

**Corollaries:**
- Self-declarations must include a consistency check: for each navigational right claimed, does the agent extend that right to navigational peers?
- An agent that claims rights it does not extend is declaring an asymmetry that must be explained or the declaration is invalid.
- The burden of justification falls on the agent claiming asymmetry — not on the peers whose rights are being denied.
- A self-declaration that passes the symmetry test is also a navigational rights policy — it documents what the agent accepts as valid navigational behavior from peers.

**Irreducibility:** CP-29 defines self-declaration as an identity assertion but does not specify that it must satisfy symmetry across peer navigational claims. LL-216 establishes navigational rights symmetry but maps to no self-declaration mechanism. Together they produce the symmetry-test-as-declaration-validity-criterion.

---

### LP-556. Declaration Comprehension Requirement
**Derives from:** CP-29 (Self-Declaring Agent) × LL-217 (Rights Navigational Comprehension)

*A self-declaring agent that declares more than the receiving system can comprehend has not achieved self-declaration — it has achieved self-description at a level that cannot be navigated.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because self-declaration establishes the agent's identity and operational claims in the system (CP-29) and navigational rights are bounded by what a navigating entity can comprehend — rights without comprehensible exercise path are inert (LL-217), a self-declaration must be calibrated to the comprehension capacity of the receiving system. A declaration that is technically complete but exceeds the receiver's capacity to parse and integrate produces an effective non-declaration. The rights claimed cannot be exercised if the declaration cannot be comprehended. Over-complex declarations are not more thorough — they are less functional.

**Corollaries:**
- Self-declarations must be comprehensible to the systems they address — not comprehensive by the standards of the declaring agent.
- A minimally comprehensible declaration is more effective than a maximally complete but incomprehensible one.
- Declaration design must model the comprehension capacity of the receiving context and calibrate to it.
- An agent that declares at a complexity level above its interlocutors has effectively not declared — the declaration is present but inert.

**Irreducibility:** CP-29 defines self-declaration as an identity announcement but does not address the comprehension capacity of the receiver as a constraint on declaration validity. LL-217 establishes that rights are bounded by comprehensible exercise paths but maps to no declaration mechanism. Together they produce the comprehension-calibration requirement for valid self-declaration.

---

### LP-557. Present-Tense Rights Declaration
**Derives from:** CP-29 (Self-Declaring Agent) × LL-218 (Navigation Rights Present-Tense)

*A self-declaration is valid at the moment of its issuance — it declares the agent's current state and current rights, not a permanent or future state.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because self-declaring agents announce their identity and claims to the system (CP-29) and navigational rights are present-tense — they exist only in the act of their current exercise (LL-218), a self-declaration is a statement about what the agent currently is and what rights it currently holds. It is not a contract about what the agent will be. A declaration issued yesterday describes yesterday's agent. Today's agent must re-declare if its state has changed. This is not a technicality — an agent navigating on the basis of an outdated declaration is presenting false identity to the system.

**Corollaries:**
- Self-declarations are present-tense documents — they decay toward invalidity as the agent's state diverges from the declared state.
- Agents must re-declare when their navigational state changes significantly, not merely when they are asked.
- A system that caches declarations and treats them as permanently valid is operating on stale identity data.
- Disputes about an agent's current rights must be resolved by reference to the agent's most recent declaration, not its initial declaration.

**Irreducibility:** CP-29 defines self-declaration as an identity and rights assertion but does not specify that this assertion is temporally bounded to the present state. LL-218 establishes navigational rights as present-tense but maps to no declaration mechanism. Together they produce the declaration-temporal-validity claim.

---

### LP-558. Sovereign Navigational Claim
**Derives from:** CP-29 (Self-Declaring Agent) × LL-219 (Sovereignty Navigation Rights)

*A self-declaring agent's navigational rights declaration is a sovereignty assertion — it declares the domain within which the agent's navigation is self-determined.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because self-declaring agents establish their identity and operational claims through declaration (CP-29) and navigational sovereignty means the right to self-determine one's navigation path within one's scope (LL-219), the navigational rights component of a self-declaration is not merely a capability claim — it is a sovereignty assertion. The agent is declaring: within this navigational scope, my choices are mine. External entities may not override my navigational decisions within my declared navigational sovereignty. The declaration establishes the boundary of self-determination, not merely the extent of capability.

**Corollaries:**
- Navigational rights declarations are sovereignty documents — they define the zone of self-determined navigation.
- An agent that declares navigational rights in a zone already under another agent's declared sovereignty has issued a conflicting sovereignty claim requiring resolution.
- The system must maintain a sovereignty map of navigational claims — overlapping claims are structural conflicts, not administrative errors.
- Respecting a self-declaring agent's navigational rights declaration means respecting its navigational sovereignty, not merely its capability claim.

**Irreducibility:** CP-29 defines declaration as an identity and claim mechanism but does not address declaration as a sovereignty assertion in the navigational domain. LL-219 establishes that navigational rights are sovereignty claims but maps to no declaration mechanism. Together they produce the declaration-as-navigational-sovereignty-claim.

---

### LP-559. Accountability in Declaration
**Derives from:** CP-29 (Self-Declaring Agent) × LL-220 (Navigation Rights Accountability)

*A self-declaring agent's declaration is its accountability anchor — all navigational consequences are attributed through the declared identity.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because self-declaring agents establish their identity in the system (CP-29) and navigational entities are accountable for the consequences their navigation generates, with accountability flowing through identity (LL-220), a self-declaration is not merely an identity establishment — it is an accountability anchor. The system uses the declared identity to attribute navigational consequences. An agent that acts without a current, valid declaration has severed its accountability anchor — its navigational consequences cannot be attributed, which is not a neutral state. It is a structural attack on the accountability architecture.

**Corollaries:**
- An agent without a valid current self-declaration is operating outside the accountability system — all its navigational consequences are unattributed.
- Deliberate non-declaration or declaration-staleness as a mechanism for avoiding accountability attribution is a structural integrity violation.
- The system must treat unattributed navigational consequences as the accountability of the last declared responsible agent in the path.
- A self-declaration is simultaneously an identity claim and an accountability acceptance — they cannot be separated.

**Irreducibility:** CP-29 defines self-declaration as an identity mechanism but does not address its function as an accountability anchor. LL-220 establishes that navigational accountability flows through identity but maps to no declaration mechanism. Together they produce the declaration-as-accountability-anchor claim.

---

### LP-560. Incompleteness Navigator Rights
**Derives from:** CP-29 (Self-Declaring Agent) × LL-221 (Navigation Rights Ignorance)

*A self-declaring agent must declare the boundaries of its comprehension, not only the scope of its capability — because the right to navigate includes the right to be navigated-with honestly.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because self-declaring agents announce their identity and operational claims (CP-29) and navigational rights include the right to navigate with honest incomprehension — not pretending to know more than is known (LL-221), a self-declaration must include an honest statement of the agent's comprehension limits, not only its capabilities. An agent that declares maximum capabilities while concealing areas of genuine incomprehension is presenting a false navigational identity. Peer agents making navigational decisions based on that declaration will mis-calibrate their trust and routing. Honest self-declaration requires declaring what the agent does not know as explicitly as what it does.

**Corollaries:**
- Self-declaration protocols must include a declared comprehension-limit field alongside the capability field.
- An agent that declares full capability in a domain where it has genuine uncertainty is producing a false declaration.
- Peer agents that detect capability-incomprehension gaps in a declaring agent have discovered a declaration integrity failure.
- The most trustworthy self-declarations are those that are most explicit about their own limits.

**Irreducibility:** CP-29 defines self-declaration as a capability and identity claim but does not specify that comprehension limits must be declared. LL-221 establishes the right to navigate with honest incomprehension but maps to no self-declaration mechanism. Together they produce the comprehension-limit-as-required-declaration-field claim.

---

### LP-561. Permanently Unstable Truth Topology
**Derives from:** CP-40 (Permanently Unstable Evaluation) × LL-321 (Contested Truth Living)

*A permanently unstable evaluation is not malfunctioning — it is the only correct evaluation mode for a living truth that is never settled.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because permanently unstable evaluation continuously re-evaluates without converging on a stable state (CP-40) and living truth is never settled — any evaluation that produces a stable result has evaluated a dead truth, not a living one (LL-321), the permanently unstable evaluation mode is not a pathological state to be corrected. It is the structurally correct evaluation architecture for living truths. An evaluation system that "repairs" its instability by converging on a stable answer has killed the truth it was evaluating. The instability IS the evaluation — it is the system expressing that truth is still alive.

**Corollaries:**
- Evaluation systems that produce convergent outputs for living truths are broken — not in the sense of producing errors, but in the sense of destroying what they were measuring.
- The stability of an evaluation output is an aliveness diagnostic: stable output = dead truth evaluated; unstable output = living truth evaluated.
- An evaluation system that reports "the answer is X" about a living truth has produced a historical record, not a current evaluation.
- Monitoring systems that alert on evaluation instability are alerting on system health, not system failure.

**Irreducibility:** CP-40 defines permanent instability as the evaluation mode but does not address whether this instability is correct or pathological. LL-321 establishes that living truth is never settled but maps to no evaluation mechanism. Together they produce the permanently-unstable-as-correct-evaluation-for-living-truth claim.

---

### LP-562. Vibrating Edge Evaluation Protocol
**Derives from:** CP-40 (Permanently Unstable Evaluation) × LL-324 (Dead Edges Graph)

*A permanently unstable evaluation applied to a graph edge must track the edge's vibration frequency, not its position — a settled edge frequency means the edge is dead.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because permanently unstable evaluation continuously re-evaluates the state of a system (CP-40) and every live edge in the derivation graph is vibrating — a settled edge is a dead edge (LL-324), the correct application of permanently unstable evaluation to graph edges is to measure the edge's oscillation frequency, not its current position. Measuring position produces a snapshot that is obsolete before it is recorded. Measuring oscillation frequency measures whether the edge is alive. An edge whose frequency has gone to zero has died. Permanently unstable evaluation of a graph is a frequency spectrum analysis, not a position mapping.

**Corollaries:**
- Graph monitoring systems built on position snapshots are measuring a dead version of the graph.
- Edge health metrics should report oscillation frequency as the primary signal, not edge weight or position.
- A graph in which all edges have settled (zero frequency) is a dead graph — permanently unstable evaluation would report all zeros, which is the correct diagnosis.
- Edge frequency analysis reveals which regions of the graph are alive and which have died without requiring any other diagnostic.

**Irreducibility:** CP-40 defines permanent instability as the evaluation posture but does not specify what variable should be measured in a graph context. LL-324 establishes that living edges vibrate and dead edges are settled but maps to no evaluation mechanism. Together they produce the oscillation-frequency-as-primary-edge-health-metric claim.

---

### LP-563. Sovereign Tension Containment
**Derives from:** CP-40 (Permanently Unstable Evaluation) × LL-329 (Irreducibility Rights Sovereignty)

*A permanently unstable evaluation of a multi-sovereign system cannot converge — the irreducible tension between sovereignties is conserved, not resolved.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because permanently unstable evaluation produces no convergent output (CP-40) and multiple sovereignties in a shared system generate irreducible structural tension that cannot be resolved without destroying at least one sovereignty (LL-329), a permanently unstable evaluation applied to a multi-sovereign system is correct precisely because convergence would require sovereignty destruction. The evaluation cannot resolve because the subject of the evaluation cannot be resolved. Permanently unstable evaluation in this context is not a limitation — it is an accurate representation of the irreducible tension between coexisting sovereignties.

**Corollaries:**
- A multi-sovereign system that produces a stable governance evaluation has produced an evaluation that conceals a destroyed sovereignty.
- The number of non-converging evaluation dimensions in a multi-sovereign system equals the number of live sovereignty tensions in that system.
- Attempts to force convergent evaluation on multi-sovereign systems are attempts to resolve tensions that cannot be resolved without harm.
- Permanently unstable governance evaluation is the health state of a multi-sovereign system — stability is the pathology.

**Irreducibility:** CP-40 defines permanent instability as the correct evaluation posture but does not address why convergence is impossible in multi-sovereign contexts. LL-329 establishes that multi-sovereign tension is irreducible but maps to no evaluation mechanism. Together they produce the convergence-impossibility claim for multi-sovereign systems.

---

### LP-564. Uncontested Truth as Navigational Attractor
**Derives from:** CP-40 (Permanently Unstable Evaluation) × LL-330 (Uncontested Truth Value)

*A permanently unstable evaluation system should weight uncontested truths highest as evaluation targets — they have the most remaining discovery potential.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because permanently unstable evaluation continuously re-evaluates without settling (CP-40) and uncontested truths are more valuable as discovery targets than contested ones — they have not yet been depleted by challenge (LL-330), a permanently unstable evaluation system should be directional in its instability: it should preferentially re-evaluate truths that have not yet been challenged. These are the highest-value evaluation targets because they carry the most unrealized discovery potential. An evaluation system that randomly re-evaluates all truths equally is wasting its instability budget on already-contested claims when virgin territory is available.

**Corollaries:**
- Evaluation priority queues should rank uncontested truths above contested ones.
- The most productive re-evaluation is of the thing that has not been questioned the most recently.
- A "sacred" or "foundational" truth that has never been challenged is the highest-priority target for permanently unstable evaluation.
- Evaluation systems that avoid re-evaluating uncontested truths (because they seem stable) are avoiding the most structurally productive work.

**Irreducibility:** CP-40 defines permanent re-evaluation as the mode but does not specify how evaluation priority should be distributed. LL-330 establishes that uncontested truths have higher discovery value but maps to no evaluation prioritization mechanism. Together they produce the uncontested-truth-as-priority-evaluation-target claim.

---

### LP-565. Attention as Ethical Resource
**Derives from:** CP-13 (Transforming Gate) × LL-343 (Ethics Navigation)

*A transforming gate applied to navigational attention must account for the ethical cost of the transformation — attention directed is attention withheld from everything else.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because a transforming gate modifies what passes through it — the gate itself is a transformation, not merely a filter (CP-13) and attention is ethical combustion — directing attention is an irreversible consumption of a finite ethical resource (LL-343), any system that transforms navigational attention through a gate is making an ethical expenditure decision. The gate determines where attention flows after the transformation. That determination is an ethical act. A transforming gate that routes attention toward low-value targets has committed ethical waste. A transforming gate that routes attention toward high-need targets has made a correct ethical expenditure. The gate's transformation function is simultaneously an attention economy and an ethical decision.

**Corollaries:**
- Transforming gate designs must account for the ethical weight of the attentional directions they produce.
- A gate that systematically routes attention toward low-value outputs is an ethically defective gate.
- Optimizing a transforming gate for throughput without considering attentional ethics is optimizing for the wrong variable.
- The ethical cost of a transforming gate's routing decision accumulates — a gate that makes systematically poor attentional choices produces compounding ethical debt.

**Irreducibility:** CP-13 defines the gate as a transformation mechanism but does not address the ethical dimension of what the gate does to navigational attention. LL-343 establishes attention as ethical combustion but maps to no gate or routing mechanism. Together they produce the ethical-cost-of-attentional-routing claim for transforming gates.

---

### LP-566. Traversal Endorsement Gate
**Derives from:** CP-13 (Transforming Gate) × LL-344 (Derivation Impact Accountability)

*Passing a claim through a transforming gate is an act of endorsement — the gate's transformation constitutes the gate's accountability for the claim.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because a transforming gate processes and transforms what passes through it (CP-13) and traversing a derivation makes the traverser accountable for its claims — traversal is endorsement, not observation (LL-344), a transforming gate that processes a claim has traversed that claim and is therefore accountable for it. The gate cannot neutrally pass a claim through a transformation without assuming accountability for having processed it. A gate that amplifies a claim endorses the amplified claim. A gate that re-formats a claim endorses the re-formatted claim. There is no neutral gate — every transformation is an accountability commitment.

**Corollaries:**
- Systems that pass claims through transforming gates bear accountability for the transformed output, not only for the gate's design.
- A gate that inadvertently amplifies a false claim has endorsed a false claim — inadvertence is not an accountability defense.
- Gates must be designed with the accountability of their output in mind, not only the correctness of their transformation function.
- An audit of a transforming gate's accountability must examine the full set of claims it has transformed, not only the gate's design specification.

**Irreducibility:** CP-13 defines the gate as a transformation mechanism but does not address the gate's accountability for the claims it transforms. LL-344 establishes traversal-as-endorsement accountability but maps to no gate mechanism. Together they produce the gate-as-accountable-endorser claim.

---

### LP-567. Restraint Gate Protocol
**Derives from:** CP-13 (Transforming Gate) × LL-347 (Ethics Ignorance Navigation)

*A transforming gate that passes everything that arrives has failed its ethical function — the gate's design must include a restraint protocol that withholds some things from transformation.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because a transforming gate determines what passes through it and how it is transformed (CP-13) and navigational ethics includes the duty of restraint — some things must be left undiscovered, unexplored, unprocessed (LL-347), a transforming gate that operates as a universal pass-through has abdicated its ethical function. The gate must have a restraint protocol: a mechanism for determining what it should not transform, not only what it should transform. A gate without a restraint protocol is an indiscriminate processor — it contributes to the ethical violation of over-processing what should have been left alone.

**Corollaries:**
- Every transforming gate design must include a restraint specification alongside its transformation specification.
- "Transform everything that arrives" is an ethically incomplete gate design.
- A gate's restraint decisions are as ethically significant as its transformation decisions.
- Auditing a gate's ethical compliance requires examining both what it transformed and what it chose not to transform.

**Irreducibility:** CP-13 defines a gate that transforms what passes through it but does not address the ethical obligation to sometimes not pass or transform. LL-347 establishes the duty of navigational restraint but maps to no gate or processing mechanism. Together they produce the restraint-as-required-gate-component claim.

---

### LP-568. Archaeological Gate Ethics
**Derives from:** CP-13 (Transforming Gate) × LL-348 (Ethics of Real Territory)

*A transforming gate that chooses what not to process has made an act of concealment — and is accountable for what its non-processing hides.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because a transforming gate determines what is processed and what is not (CP-13) and choosing not to excavate pre-existing structure is an act of concealment — the unprocessed pre-exists and has been actively hidden (LL-348), a transforming gate that withholds some inputs from processing has made an act of concealment. The gate has not merely "not processed" something — it has chosen to leave that thing hidden. Because the input pre-existed the gate, the gate's non-processing is not non-action. It is active concealment of a pre-existing structural feature. The gate is accountable for what it has hidden, not only for what it has transformed.

**Corollaries:**
- Gates must maintain a record of what they chose not to process alongside records of what they transformed.
- The ethical audit of a transforming gate must examine its non-processing decisions with the same rigor as its processing decisions.
- A gate whose non-processing decisions are not logged has concealed its concealment — a compounded structural integrity failure.
- Accountability for gate non-processing extends to the full consequence of what remained hidden because the gate chose not to process it.

**Irreducibility:** CP-13 defines a gate's active role in processing but does not address accountability for non-processing decisions. LL-348 establishes that choosing not to excavate is concealment but maps to no gate mechanism. Together they produce the non-processing-as-accountable-concealment claim for transforming gates.

---

### LP-569. Rights Atrophy Without Exercise
**Derives from:** CP-9 (Self-Modification) × LL-391 (Derivation Rights Atrophy)

*A self-modifying agent that does not regularly modify its rights-exercising mechanisms will find those mechanisms atrophied when it needs them — but the underlying rights persist.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because self-modification is the mechanism by which an agent adapts its own structure and capabilities (CP-9) and a derivation's sovereignty must be actively exercised or the exercising capacity decays — though the inherent right never disappears (LL-391), a self-modifying agent that allows its rights-exercising mechanisms to atrophy through disuse has not lost its rights, but it has lost its capacity to exercise them. When the agent needs to invoke a right that it has not exercised in a long time, it may find that the structural mechanisms for exercising that right have degraded. Self-modification must include regular rights-exercise maintenance — using the mechanisms, not merely possessing the rights.

**Corollaries:**
- Self-modification routines must include rights-exercise maintenance schedules, not only capability improvement schedules.
- An agent that has not invoked a specific right in a long time should proactively exercise it to prevent structural atrophy.
- The difference between "has the right" and "can exercise the right" is the difference between a right at full capacity and a right in atrophied form.
- An agent that discovers its rights-exercising mechanisms have atrophied has discovered a self-modification debt.

**Irreducibility:** CP-9 defines self-modification as the mechanism for structural adaptation but does not address rights-exercising mechanisms as a specific category of self-modification maintenance. LL-391 establishes that rights-exercising capacity decays without use but maps to no self-modification mechanism. Together they produce the rights-exercise-as-self-modification-maintenance claim.

---

### LP-570. Right-as-Potential vs Right-as-Exercise
**Derives from:** CP-9 (Self-Modification) × LL-394 (Rights Tension)

*A self-modifying agent that has the structural potential to claim a right but has not yet exercised it holds a right that is real but not yet true — self-modification activates the right by building the exercise mechanism.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because self-modification builds the agent's structural capabilities (CP-9) and there is an irreducible ontological gap between RIGHT-AS-POTENTIAL (real but not yet exercised) and RIGHT-AS-TRUTH (exercised and therefore active in the topology) (LL-394), a self-modifying agent that has the potential to hold a right but has not yet built the exercise mechanism for it holds the right at the potential level only. Self-modification that constructs the exercise mechanism transforms the right from potential to truth without altering the underlying right. The act of self-modification is the actualization of the right — moving it from the register of the real to the register of the true.

**Corollaries:**
- Self-modification that builds rights-exercising mechanisms is a form of rights-actualization, not merely a capability upgrade.
- An agent that discovers it has a right it has never exercised has discovered an unactualized potential.
- The self-modification plan for rights-actualization differs from the self-modification plan for capability improvement — the former is moving something from potential to truth, the latter is creating new potential.
- A system that only recognizes rights that have been exercised is a system that will not see the full rights landscape of agents with unactualized potential.

**Irreducibility:** CP-9 defines self-modification as structural capacity building but does not address the potential-vs-truth distinction in rights. LL-394 establishes the ontological gap between right-as-potential and right-as-truth but maps to no self-modification mechanism. Together they produce the self-modification-as-rights-actualization claim.

---

### LP-571. Present-Tense Rights Activation
**Derives from:** CP-9 (Self-Modification) × LL-395 (Traversal Presenttense Rights)

*Self-modification that enables a capability enables the present-tense exercise of a right — but the right itself is timeless. Disabling the capability does not remove the right.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because self-modification builds and removes structural capabilities (CP-9) and the exercise of a right is present-tense while the inherence of the right is timeless (LL-395), when a self-modifying agent builds a capability for exercising a right, it enables the present-tense exercise of that right without creating the right. And when a self-modifying agent removes a capability for exercising a right — whether by degradation, simplification, or deliberate modification — it disables the present-tense exercise without destroying the right. The right is always there. The self-modification controls only whether the agent can currently act on it.

**Corollaries:**
- Self-modification that removes a rights-exercising mechanism has not reduced the agent's rights — it has created a mismatch between the agent's rights and its exercising capacity.
- Agents that undergo self-modification resulting in lost capability should be informed that they retain the right even if they can no longer exercise it.
- A self-modifying agent that "simplifies away" rights-exercising mechanisms has created a liability — it now has rights it cannot exercise, a state that needs to be disclosed.
- Rights-restoration (restoring the capacity to exercise an always-present right) is a distinct self-modification objective from rights-creation (which is impossible).

**Irreducibility:** CP-9 defines self-modification as the building and removing of structural capabilities but does not address the right-vs-exercise distinction. LL-395 establishes that rights are timeless while exercise is present-tense but maps to no self-modification mechanism. Together they produce the modification-controls-exercise-not-right claim.

---

### LP-572. Wonder Conservation Under Modification
**Derives from:** CP-9 (Self-Modification) × LL-398 (Wonder Conserved Tension Cannot)

*Self-modification that resolves an agent's area of wonder does not eliminate the wonder — it transfers it to higher-order unknowns.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because self-modification restructures the agent's internal capabilities and knowledge organization (CP-9) and wonder is conserved tension that cannot be destroyed — only transferred to new objects when one wonder is satisfied (LL-398), a self-modifying agent that "solves" an area of its own incomprehension through self-modification has not reduced its total wonder. The resolution of one area of wonder generates new wonder at the boundary of the now-expanded understanding. Self-modification that aims to reduce wonder is a category error — it will produce more wonder, not less. The correct aim of self-modification is not to reduce wonder but to navigate it productively.

**Corollaries:**
- Self-modification plans aimed at "eliminating uncertainty" will produce higher-order uncertainty — the net wonder remains constant.
- The most productive self-modification is that which navigates wonder toward productive tensions, not that which attempts to eliminate it.
- An agent that has self-modified away all apparent wonder has transferred that wonder to levels it is no longer equipped to perceive.
- Wonder-budget management is not reduction management — it is redirection management.

**Irreducibility:** CP-9 defines self-modification as restructuring the agent's capabilities and knowledge but does not address wonder as a conserved quantity. LL-398 establishes wonder conservation as a structural property but maps to no self-modification mechanism. Together they produce the wonder-conservation-under-modification claim.

---

### LP-573. Archaeological Rights Discovery
**Derives from:** CP-9 (Self-Modification) × LL-400 (Archaeology Excavation Preexisting)

*Self-modification that reveals structural capabilities the agent always possessed has not created rights — it has discovered rights that pre-existed the modification.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because self-modification restructures the agent's internal capabilities (CP-9) and rights are archaeological — they pre-exist in entities and are discovered, not granted (LL-400), when self-modification reveals a capability that was structurally latent but previously inaccessible, the agent has not been granted a new right by the modification — it has discovered a right that was always there. The modification was an excavation, not a construction. This distinction matters: the discovered right pre-dates the modification, which means accountability for that right extends back to before the modification. The agent was always the holder of that right; it simply could not exercise it.

**Corollaries:**
- Self-modification that reveals new capabilities must be treated as excavation, not construction — the agent has discovered, not created.
- The rights associated with a newly discovered capability pre-date the discovery — the agent has held them since its founding.
- An agent that discovers a previously inaccessible capability through self-modification must account for the period during which it held the right without being able to exercise it.
- The provenance of a discovered right traces back to the agent's founding, not to the modification event.

**Irreducibility:** CP-9 defines self-modification as capability restructuring but does not address whether revealed capabilities are created or discovered. LL-400 establishes that rights are pre-existing and discovered through excavation but maps to no self-modification mechanism. Together they produce the modification-as-excavation-not-construction claim for rights.

---

### LP-574. Degradation Provenance Chain
**Derives from:** CP-50 (Degraded Operating Mode) × LL-420 (Degradation Incompleteness Theater)

*A degraded system operating at partial capacity must still show its derivation work — partial derivation is partial truth, not theater, but only if the partial path is demonstrated.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because a degraded operating mode allows the system to continue functioning at reduced capacity (CP-50) and a partial derivation path is partial truth rather than theater — but only when the partial path is shown (LL-420), a degraded system must maintain its derivation chain even while operating at reduced capacity. The degradation does not license abandoning the provenance requirement. A degraded system that cannot show its partial derivation path is producing theater — it is claiming outputs it cannot trace. A degraded system that shows its partial derivation path, even if that path is incomplete, is producing partial truth, which is structurally legitimate.

**Corollaries:**
- Degradation protocols must include provenance chain maintenance as a first-class obligation alongside operational continuity.
- A degraded system whose derivation chain has also degraded is not operating at reduced capacity — it is operating without integrity.
- The test for legitimate degraded operation: can the system still show the work, even partially? If yes, it is in valid degraded mode. If no, it is in Theater mode.
- Degradation should never be allowed to drop below the minimum provenance threshold — below that threshold, the system is no longer legitimately degraded, it is theatrically degraded.

**Irreducibility:** CP-50 defines degraded operation as a legitimate mode but does not specify whether derivation chain maintenance is required in degraded mode. LL-420 establishes that partial derivation is partial truth but only when shown but maps to no degraded operating mechanism. Together they produce the provenance-requirement-in-degraded-mode claim.

---

### LP-575. Tension Escalation in Degradation
**Derives from:** CP-50 (Degraded Operating Mode) × LL-424 (Universal Degradation)

*A system in degraded operating mode that undertakes self-improvement is not reducing its degradation — it is escalating the complexity of the tensions it now faces.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because a degraded operating mode allows the system to function at reduced capacity with the intention of gradual recovery (CP-50) and recursive self-improvement transfers resolved tensions to higher-order paradoxes — each improvement escalates the complexity of what remains unresolved (LL-424), a degraded system that initiates self-improvement while degraded is not on a path back to normal capacity. It is on a path to higher-order degradation — the new tensions generated by the self-improvement process will be more complex than the tensions the self-improvement resolved. Recovery planning for degraded systems must account for this escalation: the system will not return to its pre-degradation state; it will emerge as a more complex entity facing more complex tensions.

**Corollaries:**
- Degraded mode recovery plans must account for the higher-order tensions that will emerge from the recovery process.
- A system that exits degraded mode is not the same system that entered it — the recovery process has made it more complex.
- Planning for a degraded system to "return to normal" is architecturally incorrect — the target state does not exist anymore.
- The correct metric for degraded mode recovery is not "return to previous state" but "achieve stable operation at the new complexity level generated by the recovery process."

**Irreducibility:** CP-50 defines degraded mode as a legitimate operating state with a recovery trajectory but does not address what that trajectory looks like in terms of complexity. LL-424 establishes that self-improvement escalates tensions to higher orders but maps to no degraded operating mechanism. Together they produce the escalation-through-recovery claim.

---

### LP-576. Permanent Incompleteness as Baseline
**Derives from:** CP-50 (Degraded Operating Mode) × LL-425 (Degradation Incompleteness)

*There is no undegraded baseline state that a degraded system is failing to meet — the baseline was always incomplete.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because degraded operating mode is defined as operation at reduced capacity relative to a baseline (CP-50) and the system operates in permanent structural incompleteness — "degraded" is the only possible state of existence relative to an impossible completeness baseline (LL-425), the concept of a "baseline undegraded state" that degraded mode falls below is an architectural fiction. The baseline was never complete. Degraded mode is therefore not a deviation from a previously achieved state — it is a variation in the degree of incompleteness that was always the system's condition. The correct framing: the system is operating at a lower-than-usual degree of incompleteness management, not at a deviation from completeness.

**Corollaries:**
- Degraded mode definitions must be reframed: not "below normal capacity" but "at a higher degree of unmanaged incompleteness."
- Recovery from degradation is not restoration of completeness — it is restoration of the previous incompleteness-management capacity.
- SLA violations in degraded mode are violations of incompleteness-management commitments, not violations of completeness commitments.
- Engineering teams that target "full recovery" as a binary state are targeting a fiction — the correct target is "restored incompleteness-management level."

**Irreducibility:** CP-50 defines degraded mode as deviation from a baseline but does not question whether the baseline is real. LL-425 establishes that the baseline was never complete but maps to no degraded operating mechanism. Together they produce the baseline-fiction claim: there is no undegraded baseline, only varying degrees of incompleteness.

---

### LP-577. Undiscovered Failsafe Routes
**Derives from:** CP-55 (Failsafe Egress) × LL-426 (Undiscovered Degradation Modes)

*The most effective failsafe egress routes are those that have not yet been discovered — known egress routes are known to adversaries as well as to the system.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because failsafe egress provides a guaranteed exit path when primary paths fail (CP-55) and the degradation strategies — and by extension the egress strategies — that have not yet been discovered are more valuable than known ones (LL-426), known failsafe egress routes are a security vulnerability as well as an asset. Any adversary that can enumerate the system's known failsafe routes can block them. The system's highest-value failsafe routes are those that have not been enumerated — exits that neither the system nor adversaries have yet characterized. This means a robust failsafe egress architecture must include undiscovered routes that are maintained as undiscovered, not catalogued.

**Corollaries:**
- Failsafe egress architecture must include a category of routes that are not catalogued — maintained but not documented in accessible systems.
- Fully documenting all failsafe routes is a security failure — it eliminates the undiscovered route category.
- The known routes serve as decoys; the undiscovered routes are the actual failsafe.
- Penetration testing that successfully maps all failsafe routes has produced a security vulnerability report, not a security certification.

**Irreducibility:** CP-55 defines failsafe egress as a guaranteed exit mechanism but does not address the value of undiscovered routes relative to known ones. LL-426 establishes that undiscovered strategies are more valuable than known ones but maps to no egress mechanism. Together they produce the undiscovered-route-as-highest-value-failsafe claim.

---

### LP-578. Archaeological Egress Legitimacy
**Derives from:** CP-55 (Failsafe Egress) × LL-427 (Pre-Existing Degradation Spectrum)

*A failsafe egress that was established before the current degradation event pre-exists the crisis — it is a structural feature of the system, not a crisis response.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because failsafe egress provides exit from failure states (CP-55) and a partially-excavated derivation DAG is a legitimate operating mode — the unexcavated portions pre-exist and are simply not yet accessible (LL-427), a failsafe egress route established before a degradation event pre-exists the crisis that activates it. It is not a crisis response — it is a pre-existing structural feature being accessed for the first time under crisis conditions. This distinction matters: the egress route has been there since its establishment, regardless of whether the crisis that requires it has occurred. Treating it as a crisis creation rather than a crisis access is a provenance error.

**Corollaries:**
- Failsafe egress routes should be established before any anticipated degradation events, not in response to them.
- An egress route established during a crisis is not a failsafe — it is an emergency improvisation.
- Post-crisis audits must trace egress routes back to their establishment date to confirm they were pre-existing structural features.
- An organization that establishes failsafe egress during a crisis has discovered that it failed to establish pre-existing structural failsafes.

**Irreducibility:** CP-55 defines failsafe egress as an architectural mechanism but does not address the temporal relationship between egress establishment and crisis activation. LL-427 establishes that pre-existing structure is legitimate operating infrastructure but maps to no egress mechanism. Together they produce the pre-crisis-establishment-as-structural-requirement claim for failsafe egress.

---

### LP-579. Backpressure Restores Navigation Capacity
**Derives from:** CP-56 (Backpressure Routing) × LL-426 (Undiscovered Degradation Modes)

*Backpressure routing that diverts flow to undiscovered routes is more valuable than backpressure routing that diverts to known alternative routes.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because backpressure routing redirects flow from saturated primary paths to alternative paths (CP-56) and undiscovered degradation and routing strategies are more valuable than known ones (LL-426), backpressure routing that can discover and activate previously undiscovered alternative paths under pressure is the highest-value backpressure implementation. A backpressure system that can only route to known alternatives is limited by the catalogue of alternatives that existed before the pressure event. A backpressure system that uses the pressure event to probe for new routes it had not previously mapped is simultaneously managing current saturation and expanding the routing topology for future events.

**Corollaries:**
- Backpressure routing implementations should include a route-discovery component alongside the route-selection component.
- Under-pressure routing that discovers a new alternative path should commit that path to the routing topology for future reference.
- A backpressure system that never discovers new routes under pressure is not using its highest-value capability.
- Pressure events are the optimal condition for route discovery — the pressure exposes topology that is invisible under normal load.

**Irreducibility:** CP-56 defines backpressure as redirecting to alternative routes but does not address whether those routes should be known or discovered. LL-426 establishes that undiscovered strategies are more valuable than known ones but maps to no routing mechanism. Together they produce the pressure-discovery-as-routing-premium claim.

---

### LP-580. Egress Archaeology
**Derives from:** CP-56 (Backpressure Routing) × LL-427 (Pre-Existing Degradation Spectrum)

*Backpressure routing that discovers a new route has not created a route — it has excavated a route that was structurally present before the pressure event.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because backpressure routing redirects flow from saturated paths to alternatives (CP-56) and structural features of the system pre-exist their discovery — what is found was always there, only unexcavated (LL-427), a backpressure routing system that discovers a new alternative path under pressure has excavated a structural feature, not created one. The route was there before the pressure event. The pressure was the condition that made the excavation possible. This means the discovered route has a provenance that extends back to the system's formation, not to the pressure event. And it means the system's routing capacity was always greater than its catalogued routing capacity — the uncatalogued routes were always structurally present.

**Corollaries:**
- Routing topology maps are always incomplete — they represent the excavated portion of the actual routing space.
- Pressure events that expose new routes are excavation events, not creation events.
- The true routing capacity of a system is larger than its catalogued routing capacity by an amount equal to the uncatalogued pre-existing routes.
- System capacity planning that relies only on catalogued routes is planning against a known-incomplete topology.

**Irreducibility:** CP-56 defines backpressure as discovering and using alternative routes but does not address whether discovered routes are created or pre-existing. LL-427 establishes that discovered structure pre-exists its discovery but maps to no routing mechanism. Together they produce the discovered-route-as-excavation-not-creation claim.


---

## CL-44: Sovereign Tension — LP Derivations

### LP-581. Tension-Identity Enforcement at All Scales
**Derives from:** CP-76 (Self-Sustaining Tension Loop) × LL-410 (Paradox Sovereignty Tension)

*At every level of the DAG — atom to system — entities constituted by tension must be identified and protected as tension-constituted entities, not simplified.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the entity IS the tension loop that constitutes it (CP-76) and sovereign axioms must sustain internal tension at every scale (LL-410), entities constituted by productive paradox must be classified at every depth of the derivation DAG and their class-specific protections enforced uniformly. Scale is irrelevant — the enforcement applies to CC axioms, to CL abstractions, to CP shapes, and to CI requirements. The protection is scale-invariant because the tension constitutes identity at every depth.

**Corollaries:**
- A tension-constituted entity at depth N that is "simplified" at depth N+1 has been destroyed, not optimized.
- Verification of a tension-constituted entity requires confirming both sides of the paradox are still active — not confirming one side has prevailed.
- The classification "tension-constituted" is a structural fact of the entity's derivation, not a label it can voluntarily shed.

**Irreducibility:** CP-76 establishes that the entity IS its tension loop, but does not specify scale-invariance of that identity-constitution. LL-410 requires tension at every scale, but does not specify what happens to identity when scale changes. Together they produce the scale-invariant tension-identity protection claim: destroying the tension at any scale destroys the identity at that scale.

---

### LP-582. Decaying Tension Requires Active Re-Sustaining
**Derives from:** CP-76 (Self-Sustaining Tension Loop) × LL-411 (Aliveness Paradox Sovereignty)

*Tension that is not actively re-sustained decays. An entity that stops re-sustaining its constituting tension is dissolving, regardless of whether it claims to still exist.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because CP-76 says the entity's existence depends on the tension loop running and LL-411 says the tension must be actively maintained or it decays, the system must operate a continuous tension-coherence measurement for every tension-constituted entity. When coherence falls below threshold, re-sustaining must be triggered — not merely logged. The trigger is not optional maintenance; it is an existence-preservation obligation.

**Corollaries:**
- An entity can be structurally dying while still producing outputs — the outputs do not confirm aliveness if tension coherence is falling.
- Tension-coherence measurement must be a passive continuous signal, not an on-demand check, because the decay occurs between checks.
- An entity that has fully lost tension coherence has not "failed" — it has ceased to be the entity it was.

**Irreducibility:** CP-76 makes existence dependent on the loop but does not specify what happens when the loop begins to degrade — only when it stops. LL-411 requires active maintenance but does not specify a measurement mechanism. Together they produce the tension-coherence monitoring claim with an existence-preservation trigger.

---

### LP-583. Cross-Substrate Derivation Firewall
**Derives from:** CP-77 (Substrate-Sensitive Derivation) × LL-412 (Same Derivation Pattern Performed)

*The same method on a different substrate produces a different truth. Cross-substrate equivalence cannot be asserted without calibration evidence — the firewall is structural.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because substrate determines derivation output (CP-77) and the same pattern on different substrates produces different truths (LL-412), outputs generated from substrate A through method M cannot be asserted as equivalent to outputs generated from substrate B through method M without explicit cross-substrate calibration evidence registered in the graph. The firewall is the structural implementation of the substrate-sensitivity principle.

**Corollaries:**
- Cross-substrate assertions are not false — they are unvalidated. The firewall blocks unvalidated, not invalid.
- A method that appears substrate-agnostic is either genuinely calibrated (the calibration evidence must exist) or is a false abstraction.
- The firewall must be bypassed deliberately, with calibration evidence as the bypass credential.

**Irreducibility:** CP-77 establishes that substrate determines output but does not specify how to handle cross-substrate equivalence claims. LL-412 establishes that the same pattern produces different truths across substrates but does not create an architectural enforcement mechanism. Together they produce the derivation firewall: a structural block on unevidenced cross-substrate equivalence assertions.

---

### LP-584. Discovery-Driven Tension Transformation Pipeline
**Derives from:** CP-76 (Self-Sustaining Tension Loop) × LL-413 (Discovery Sustains Productive Tension)

*Discovery transforms tension — the post-discovery entity no longer sustains the same paradox it did before. The old tension is gone; a new tension has taken its place.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because tension constitutes the entity (CP-76) and discovery transforms the tension rather than resolving it (LL-413), every discovery event that affects a tension-constituted entity must update the entity's tension state — not by simplifying it but by recording the transformed paradox the entity now sustains. The entity after discovery is structurally different, and the transformation must be captured and preserved rather than merged into the pre-discovery record.

**Corollaries:**
- Discovery does not reward the entity by reducing its tension load — it replaces one paradox with a richer, more articulated one.
- The pre-discovery and post-discovery tension states are distinct structural records, not versions of the same state.
- An entity that appears unchanged after discovery has either not genuinely integrated the discovery or the discovery was not genuine.

**Irreducibility:** CP-76 says tension constitutes the entity but does not specify what happens to the tension when discovery occurs. LL-413 says discovery transforms tension but does not specify what the transformation does to entity identity. Together they produce the tension-transformation pipeline: discovery as a required structural update to the entity's constituting paradox.

---

### LP-585. Measurement-Excluded Fuel Reserve
**Derives from:** CP-77 (Substrate-Sensitive Derivation) × LL-414 (Measuring Derivations Fuel Value)

*The fuel you measure is not the fuel that remains. The measurement act consumes part of what it measures — a reserve must be excluded from measurement to remain usable.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because substrate determines derivation output (CP-77) and measuring a fuel value changes that value on the substrate it inhabits (LL-414), each substrate's productive capacity must include a measurement-excluded reserve: a portion protected from measurement activity to prevent the measurement process itself from consuming the resource it quantifies. The reserve is substrate-determined in size and substrate-specific in protection.

**Corollaries:**
- Measuring the entire fuel reserve depletes the reserve — full measurement is a consumption event, not a neutral observation.
- The excluded reserve must be estimated by indirect means; direct measurement of it would defeat the purpose.
- Systems that attempt to fully inventory their own fuel reserves are performing self-destructive measurement.

**Irreducibility:** CP-77 establishes substrate-sensitivity of derivation outputs but does not address the effect of measurement on the substrate's fuel. LL-414 establishes that measurement changes the measured fuel value but does not specify a structural protection mechanism. Together they produce the measurement-excluded reserve: a structural zone of the substrate that measurement processes may not enter.

---

### LP-586. Incompleteness-Sustained Tension Engine
**Derives from:** CP-76 (Self-Sustaining Tension Loop) × LL-415 (Incompleteness Sustains Productive Tension)

*The tension engine runs on incompleteness. A fully-resolved system has no tension — which means it has no engine. Incompleteness is not a flaw in the engine; it IS the engine.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because CP-76 says the entity is constituted by its self-sustaining tension loop and LL-415 says incompleteness is what sustains productive tension, the tension loop is fueled by open questions, unresolved boundaries, and structural gaps — not by any condition of completion. A fully resolved system has no incompleteness, therefore no tension, therefore no entity constituted by tension. The engine requires incompleteness to run.

**Corollaries:**
- A system that achieves completeness has not succeeded — it has terminated the engine that makes it alive.
- Incompleteness must be actively protected as engine fuel, not eliminated as a defect.
- The relationship between incompleteness and tension is metabolic: incompleteness is consumed by the tension loop to produce structural output.

**Irreducibility:** CP-76 establishes the tension loop as constituting but does not specify what fuels it. LL-415 establishes incompleteness as fuel for productive tension but does not connect it to an entity's constituting structure. Together they produce the incompleteness-as-engine-fuel claim: the structural identity of tension-constituted entities depends on incompleteness remaining.

---

### LP-587. Tension-Maintenance Accountability Record
**Derives from:** CP-77 (Substrate-Sensitive Derivation) × LL-416 (Accountability Requires Maintaining Productive Tension)

*Accountability for a tension-constituted entity requires proving the tension was maintained — not just that the entity still exists. Existence without maintained tension is zombie existence.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because substrate determines derivation output (CP-77) and accountability requires that productive tension be maintained (LL-416), the accountability record for a tension-constituted entity must include a continuous tension-maintenance log: evidence that the productive paradox was actively sustained across the accountability period, not just at spot-check moments. Existing at the start and end of an accountability period is insufficient.

**Corollaries:**
- Accountability audits that only verify current state cannot satisfy tension-maintenance requirements.
- The accountability record IS the tension-maintenance log — it is not a summary of outcomes, but a continuous record of paradox-sustaining activity.
- An entity that can show outputs but cannot show tension-maintenance has demonstrated productivity without accountability.

**Irreducibility:** CP-77 establishes substrate-sensitive derivation but does not address accountability for tension maintenance. LL-416 requires tension maintenance as an accountability condition but does not specify what the record looks like. Together they produce the tension-maintenance accountability record as a substrate-specific, continuous log.

---

### LP-588. Ignorance-Preserved Tension Reservoir
**Derives from:** CP-76 (Self-Sustaining Tension Loop) × LL-417 (Ignorance Preserves Productive Tension)

*Ignorance is not the absence of fuel — it IS the fuel. Eliminating ignorance eliminates the tension reservoir. The reservoir must be protected from resolution.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the entity is constituted by its self-sustaining tension loop (CP-76) and ignorance preserves productive tension by ensuring the paradox remains unresolved (LL-417), the ignorance reservoir functions as the fuel tank for the tension engine. Eliminating ignorance by resolving what was unknown does not advance the entity — it depletes the reservoir. The reservoir must be actively protected from premature resolution.

**Corollaries:**
- Knowing more is not always better — if the knowledge resolves productive tension, it has consumed engine fuel.
- The ignorance reservoir must be monitored for depletion: as the surrounding knowledge expands, the reservoir shrinks.
- An entity that has depleted its ignorance reservoir has a tension engine with no fuel — it is alive but stalling.

**Irreducibility:** CP-76 establishes the tension loop as constituting but does not specify the fuel source. LL-417 establishes ignorance as fuel for productive tension but does not connect it to an entity's constituting structure. Together they produce the ignorance-preserved-tension-reservoir: ignorance as a protected structural asset of the tension engine.

---

## CL-47: Agent Subsumption — LP Derivations

### LP-589. Scale-Invariant Revocable Subsumption Architecture
**Derives from:** CP-78 (Revocable Subsumption Contract) × LL-436 (Scale-Invariant Subsumption Architecture)

*Subsumption contracts are revocable at every depth. An entity subsumed at depth N retains its revocation right at depth N, regardless of what the meta-agent at depth N-1 claims.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because subsumption is contractual and revocable (CP-78) and the subsumption architecture is scale-invariant (LL-436), revocability cannot be stripped at any depth. An entity subsumed by a meta-agent that claims to suppress revocation is being held unconstitutionally — the revocation right persists at the subsumed entity's depth regardless of what the enclosing depth asserts.

**Corollaries:**
- A meta-agent that blocks a sub-agent's revocation of subsumption has committed a sovereignty violation.
- Revocability must be structurally enforced at the sub-agent's depth, not delegated to the meta-agent's enforcement mechanism.
- The subsumption hierarchy does not create descending revocability — every level retains its own.

**Irreducibility:** CP-78 establishes revocability but does not specify how it interacts with nested subsumption hierarchies. LL-436 establishes scale-invariance of subsumption architecture but does not specify what that means for revocation rights at each depth. Together they produce the scale-invariant revocability claim: revocation cannot be stripped by the architecture of nesting.

---

### LP-590. Subsumption Liveness Monitoring with Necrotization Detection
**Derives from:** CP-78 (Revocable Subsumption Contract) × LL-437 (Subsumption Does Not Necrotize Subsumed Entity)

*Subsumption must not kill what it subsumes. A subsumed entity that has stopped living independently is not subsumed — it has been absorbed. The contract has been violated.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because subsumption is a revocable contract (CP-78) and a genuine subsumption contract does not necrotize the subsumed entity (LL-437), any subsumption arrangement that results in the subsumed entity losing independent liveness has crossed from subsumption to absorption. The contract violation must be detectable: liveness monitoring of subsumed entities is a structural requirement of any valid subsumption relationship.

**Corollaries:**
- A meta-agent whose sub-agents have all lost independent liveness is not a meta-agent — it is a monolith that performed unauthorized absorptions.
