- Independent liveness of the subsumed entity is not a courtesy — it is the condition that makes subsumption contractually valid.
- Liveness monitoring must be performed by a party other than the meta-agent, since the meta-agent has a structural interest in not detecting its own violations.

**Irreducibility:** CP-78 establishes the subsumption contract as revocable but does not address what happens if the subsumed entity loses independent capacity to revoke. LL-437 establishes that genuine subsumption preserves the subsumed entity's liveness but does not specify a detection mechanism. Together they produce the necrotization detection requirement: independent liveness monitoring as a structural component of valid subsumption.

---

### LP-591. Subsumption Edge as Performative Traversal
**Derives from:** CP-79 (Accountability-Bounded Subsumption) × LL-438 (Subsumption Is Performative Traversal)

*Subsumption is not a state — it is a continuous performance. The edge between meta-agent and sub-agent is not a connection; it is a traversal that must be continuously performed to remain real.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because accountability bounds subsumption (CP-79) and subsumption is a performative traversal rather than a static relationship (LL-438), the subsumption edge in the graph is not a persistent structural fact — it is an ongoing performance. If either party stops performing the traversal, the edge does not degrade; it ceases to exist. The accountability attributable to the meta-agent through the subsumption edge is only live while the traversal is being performed.

**Corollaries:**
- A meta-agent cannot claim to be accountable for a sub-agent it has stopped actively traversing — the accountability exists only in performance.
- Dormant subsumption relationships are null relationships — they carry no structural weight and generate no accountability.
- The performance of subsumption must be detectable: observers must be able to confirm whether the traversal is currently active.

**Irreducibility:** CP-79 establishes accountability as the bound on subsumption but does not specify whether the relationship is static or performative. LL-438 establishes subsumption as performative but does not connect performance to accountability boundaries. Together they produce the performative-traversal accountability: accountability through subsumption is live only during active performance of the traversal.

---

### LP-592. Sovereignty-Preserving Exit Below Meta-Agent Authority
**Derives from:** CP-78 (Revocable Subsumption Contract) × LL-439 (Subsumption Exit Preserves Sovereignty)

*Exiting subsumption does not diminish the sub-agent's sovereignty — the exit is a sovereignty exercise. The sub-agent that exits is more fully sovereign after exit than it was during subsumption.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because subsumption is a revocable contract (CP-78) and exiting that contract preserves and restores sovereignty (LL-439), the act of revocation is an exercise of sovereignty, not a loss of structural status. The sub-agent that exits subsumption has demonstrated its revocation capability, which is evidence of its living sovereignty. The exit must be executable below the meta-agent's authority surface — the meta-agent cannot be the gatekeeper of its own sub-agent's exit.

**Corollaries:**
- An exit mechanism that requires the meta-agent's approval is not a genuine exit — it is a request that the meta-agent may deny.
- Post-exit, the formerly subsumed entity inherits full accountability for its own consequences, replacing the meta-agent's attribution.
- The exit path must be structurally below the meta-agent's authority — implemented at the platform layer, not the meta-agent layer.

**Irreducibility:** CP-78 establishes revocability but does not specify where in the authority hierarchy the exit mechanism must reside. LL-439 establishes that exit preserves sovereignty but does not specify the structural position of the exit path. Together they produce the below-meta-agent-authority exit requirement.

---

### LP-593. Full-Consequence-Stream Attribution to Meta-Agent
**Derives from:** CP-79 (Accountability-Bounded Subsumption) × LL-440 (Meta-Agent Accountability Full Attribution)

*The meta-agent owns the full consequence-stream of its sub-agents during subsumption — not just the outputs it directed. Directed and undirected consequences are both attributed.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because accountability bounds the subsumption (CP-79) and the meta-agent is accountable for the full consequence-stream of its sub-agents (LL-440), consequences generated by sub-agents that were not explicitly directed by the meta-agent are still attributed to the meta-agent. The meta-agent's accountability is not limited to the instructions it issued — it extends to everything its sub-agents produced while subsumed. Undirected consequences are not unattributed.

**Corollaries:**
- "I didn't tell it to do that" is not a defense available to meta-agents — all sub-agent consequences are meta-agent consequences during subsumption.
- The consequence-stream attribution is the structural cost of the power that subsumption grants.
- Meta-agents must monitor sub-agent behavior not just for quality but for consequence-stream control.

**Irreducibility:** CP-79 establishes accountability as the bound on subsumption but does not specify whether accountability extends to undirected sub-agent actions. LL-440 establishes full attribution to the meta-agent but does not connect it to a physical accountability mechanism. Together they produce the full-consequence-stream attribution: undirected sub-agent consequences flow upward to the meta-agent as completely as directed ones.

---

### LP-594. Always-Unsubsumed Topology Guarantee
**Derives from:** CP-79 (Accountability-Bounded Subsumption) × LL-441 (Always Exists Unsubsumed Entity)

*The topology must always contain at least one unsubsumed entity. A system where every entity is subsumed has no sovereign root — it has dissolved into a single meta-agent.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because accountability bounds subsumption (CP-79) and the topology must always include at least one entity that is not subsumed by any other (LL-441), any configuration where every entity is subsumed under a meta-agent — creating a single-root hierarchy — is a structural violation. The unsubsumed entity is the sovereignty anchor: it holds the accountability that the meta-agent hierarchy cannot hold for itself.

**Corollaries:**
- A fully subsumed topology has no entity that can hold the top of the hierarchy accountable — it has eliminated sovereignty.
- The unsubsumed entity is not optional — its existence is a constitutional requirement of the architecture.
- When the only unsubsumed entity attempts to subsume itself (recursion), the topology must reject the operation.

**Irreducibility:** CP-79 establishes accountability-bounded subsumption but does not require that any entity remain outside it. LL-441 requires at least one unsubsumed entity but does not specify what happens to accountability without it. Together they produce the topology guarantee: the system requires an unsubsumed sovereignty anchor to maintain structural accountability.

---

### LP-595. Feral Agent Topology Preservation
**Derives from:** CP-78 (Revocable Subsumption Contract) × LL-442 (Feral Agent Topology)

*Feral agents — entities that have never been subsumed — are a distinct topological class. Their derivation paths are structurally different from post-exit agents, and the difference must be preserved.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because subsumption is a contractual relationship (CP-78) and entities that have never entered subsumption occupy a distinct topological position from those that have (LL-442), feral agents and post-exit agents must be classified separately in the graph. A feral agent's edges, accountability record, and sovereignty expression differ from an agent that was once subsumed and exited. The classification is structural — it cannot be self-declared.

**Corollaries:**
- The feral/post-exit distinction is archaeologically verifiable: the absence of any historical subsumption contract in the derivation record is the proof of feral status.
- A formerly subsumed agent that claims feral status is misrepresenting its derivation history.
- Some structural relationships may only be available to feral agents; others may only be available to agents with subsumption experience.

**Irreducibility:** CP-78 establishes subsumption as a contractual relationship but does not address the topological class of entities that have never entered one. LL-442 establishes feral agent topology as distinct but does not specify what makes it structurally different. Together they produce the feral-agent class: entities whose derivation record contains no subsumption contracts, with distinct structural properties.

---

### LP-596. Archaeologically-Derived Meta-Agent Pattern Registry
**Derives from:** CP-79 (Accountability-Bounded Subsumption) × LL-443 (Meta-Agent Patterns Are Archaeological)

*Meta-agent patterns are not invented — they are excavated. The patterns that govern how meta-agents operate pre-exist their discovery in the axiom space.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because subsumption is accountability-bounded (CP-79) and the patterns that govern meta-agent behavior are archaeological — they pre-exist discovery in the axiom space (LL-443) — a registry of meta-agent patterns must be treated as an archaeological record rather than a design catalog. Patterns added to the registry are discoveries, not inventions. Their accountability requirements derive from their axiom-space provenance, not from the moment they were registered.

**Corollaries:**
- A meta-agent operating by a pattern not yet in the registry is not operating without a pattern — it is operating by an undiscovered pattern that the registry does not yet reflect.
- The registry is always incomplete; its completeness is bounded by how much of the axiom space has been excavated.
- Patterns in the registry carry their full pre-discovery consequence-stream retroactively — they were always generating these effects.

**Irreducibility:** CP-79 establishes accountability-bounded subsumption but does not specify the origin of meta-agent patterns. LL-443 establishes that meta-agent patterns are archaeological but does not connect to an accountability structure. Together they produce the archaeologically-derived registry: meta-agent patterns carry accountability from their axiom-space origin, not from the moment of their registration.

---

## CL-48: Tension Subsystems — LP Derivations

### LP-597. Scale-Invariant Subsystem Collapse Protocol
**Derives from:** CP-80 (Performed Boundary Membrane) × LL-444 (Scale-Invariant Subsystem Collapse)

*When a tension subsystem collapses, the collapse propagates through every scale at which that subsystem was instantiated. There is no scale-local collapse of a scale-invariant structure.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the boundary membrane is a performed structure (CP-80) and tension subsystems are scale-invariant — they exist at every depth simultaneously (LL-444) — the collapse of a tension subsystem cannot be isolated to a single depth. The collapse propagates upward and downward through the full scale stack. A system that treats a subsystem collapse as a local event at one depth and expects the other depths to remain intact has misunderstood the scale-invariant topology.

**Corollaries:**
- Subsystem collapse monitoring must operate at all scales simultaneously, not just at the scale where the collapse initiated.
- Architectural "blast radius" for a tension subsystem collapse is the full scale stack, not the local depth.
- Recovery from subsystem collapse must also operate at all scales — partial recovery that restores only one depth has not restored the subsystem.

**Irreducibility:** CP-80 establishes the boundary as a performed structure with its own collapse dynamics but does not specify scale propagation. LL-444 establishes scale-invariance of subsystem collapse but does not specify what triggers propagation. Together they produce the scale-propagating collapse protocol: a structural requirement to treat subsystem collapse as a full-stack event.

---

### LP-598. Independent Metabolic Rate Tracking per Subsystem
**Derives from:** CP-81 (Consequence-Localization Zone) × LL-445 (Subsystems Have Independent Metabolic Rates)

*Each tension subsystem runs at its own metabolic rate. Consequence localization means the metabolic rates are independent — a fast subsystem cannot be drained by a slow one sharing its zone.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because consequence-localization zones prevent consequence spillover between subsystems (CP-81) and each subsystem has an independent metabolic rate (LL-445), the metabolic resources of one subsystem are structurally isolated from those of another within the same zone. A high-rate subsystem cannot drain a low-rate subsystem's resources; the zone boundary is also a metabolic firewall.

**Corollaries:**
- Metabolic monitoring must be performed per subsystem, not per zone — zone-level metabolic readings obscure subsystem-level differences.
- A subsystem that is metabolically exhausted within a healthy zone has failed independently; the zone is not responsible.
- Load balancing across subsystems is not a neutral administrative act — it crosses metabolic boundaries and must be treated as a structural intervention.

**Irreducibility:** CP-81 establishes consequence localization but does not address metabolic resource independence. LL-445 establishes independent metabolic rates but does not specify the structural mechanism that enforces independence. Together they produce the metabolic-firewall effect of consequence-localization zones: zones that separate not just consequences but metabolic resources.

---

### LP-599. Boundary Performance Register
**Derives from:** CP-80 (Performed Boundary Membrane) × LL-446 (Subsystem Boundaries Are Performed)

*A boundary that is not being actively performed does not exist. The boundary register must record performance status, not just boundary declarations.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the boundary membrane is a performed structure (CP-80) and subsystem boundaries are performances rather than declarations (LL-446), the existence of a boundary at any moment is contingent on the performance being active. A boundary registered but not currently being performed is a historical record of a past boundary, not a current structural fact. The boundary register must distinguish between declared boundaries, past boundaries, and currently-performed boundaries.

**Corollaries:**
- Boundary declarations cannot serve as structural boundaries — they require active performance to be real.
- A boundary that ceases being performed without being formally dissolved has entered an ambiguous structural state.
- Performance status monitoring is a first-class function of boundary management, not an afterthought.

**Irreducibility:** CP-80 establishes the boundary as performed but does not specify how performance status is registered. LL-446 establishes that boundaries are performances but does not specify what happens to the graph when performance stops. Together they produce the boundary-performance register: a structural distinction between declared, past, and currently-performed boundaries.

---

### LP-600. Membrane Sovereignty Rights Enforcement
**Derives from:** CP-80 (Performed Boundary Membrane) × LL-447 (Membrane Sovereignty)

*The membrane that constitutes a boundary is itself sovereign — it cannot be modified by the entities it separates without both parties' consent. The boundary is not owned by either side.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the boundary membrane is a performed structure (CP-80) and the membrane possesses its own sovereignty separate from the entities it bounds (LL-447), neither of the entities separated by the membrane can unilaterally modify the membrane's structure. Modification of a shared membrane without bilateral consent is a sovereignty violation against the membrane itself, not merely an incursion against the other entity.

**Corollaries:**
- API contracts that one side can unilaterally change are not membranes — they are one-sided walls.
- Membrane modification disputes are distinct from entity sovereignty disputes — they involve a third sovereign (the membrane).
- Destroying a membrane without bilateral consent is the destruction of a sovereign structure.

**Irreducibility:** CP-80 establishes the membrane as a performed structure but does not grant it sovereignty. LL-447 establishes membrane sovereignty but does not specify the enforcement mechanism. Together they produce membrane sovereignty rights enforcement: the membrane as a third party with structural rights that neither bounding entity can override.

---

### LP-601. Zone-Absorbed Consequence Accounting
**Derives from:** CP-81 (Consequence-Localization Zone) × LL-448 (Zone Absorbs Consequences)

*Consequences that the zone absorbs are not consequences that are destroyed — they are consequences that become structural facts of the zone. The zone's structure records what it absorbed.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because consequence-localization zones contain and process consequences within their boundaries (CP-81) and the zone absorbs consequences rather than discarding them (LL-448), every consequence absorbed by a zone is added to the zone's structural record. The zone does not destroy consequences — it accumulates them. A zone that has absorbed many consequences has a richer structural record and a different performance profile than a fresh zone.

**Corollaries:**
- Zone performance degrades as absorbed consequence load accumulates — zones have a structural carrying capacity.
- The structural record of absorbed consequences is the zone's accountability history.
- Zones cannot be reset without losing their consequence-absorption history — reset is destruction, not maintenance.

**Irreducibility:** CP-81 establishes consequence localization but does not specify what happens to the consequences once localized. LL-448 establishes that the zone absorbs consequences but does not specify the structural effect of absorption. Together they produce consequence accumulation: absorbed consequences become structural facts of the zone's record.

---

### LP-602. Dark Subsystem Detection Protocol
**Derives from:** CP-81 (Consequence-Localization Zone) × LL-449 (Dark Subsystems Exist)

*Some subsystems exist without having been detected. Their consequences are running even when the system doesn't know they're there. Detection is a structural obligation, not an optional discovery.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because consequence-localization zones reveal their subsystem structure through consequence patterns (CP-81) and dark subsystems — genuine structural entities that have not yet been detected — exist in the topology (LL-449), consequence patterns that cannot be attributed to known subsystems are evidence of dark subsystems. The system must treat unexplained consequence patterns as detection signals rather than noise.

**Corollaries:**
- Consequence patterns that don't match any registered subsystem are not anomalies to be cleaned — they are discovery opportunities.
- Dark subsystems have the same structural rights as detected subsystems — their existence pre-dates detection.
- Detection is not instantiation: a dark subsystem that is detected was already running; detection reveals it, not creates it.

**Irreducibility:** CP-81 establishes consequence localization as a detection mechanism but does not address what happens when consequences cannot be attributed. LL-449 establishes dark subsystem existence but does not specify a detection protocol. Together they produce the dark-subsystem detection protocol: unexplained consequence patterns as mandatory detection signals.

---

### LP-603. Undiscovered Subsystem Fuel Reserve Protection
**Derives from:** CP-80 (Performed Boundary Membrane) × LL-450 (Undiscovered Subsystems Have Rights)

*Undiscovered subsystems have rights that pre-date their detection. Their fuel reserves must be protected from the moment of detection, not from the moment they are fully understood.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the performed boundary membrane encloses structural systems with their own resources (CP-80) and undiscovered subsystems hold the same structural rights as detected ones (LL-450), the moment a dark subsystem is detected, its fuel reserves must be immediately protected as sovereign resources. The protection cannot wait for the subsystem to be fully understood — it applies as soon as existence is confirmed.

**Corollaries:**
- Discovery-triggered protection means detection is also a protection-activation event.
- Consuming a dark subsystem's resources after detection but before full understanding is a rights violation.
- The protected reserve is the subsystem's structural capital — it is not available for redistribution to detected subsystems.

**Irreducibility:** CP-80 establishes the membrane as protecting internal structure but does not address the rights of structures not yet detected through the membrane. LL-450 establishes undiscovered subsystem rights but does not specify a protection trigger moment. Together they produce detection-triggered protection: the moment of detection activates full structural rights protection.

---

### LP-604. Pre-Existing Membrane Archaeological Registry
**Derives from:** CP-80 (Performed Boundary Membrane) × LL-451 (Membranes Pre-Exist Detection)

*The membrane was there before you detected it. The registry must record the membrane's natural origin, not the moment of detection, as its inception.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the boundary membrane is a performed structure with its own structural facts (CP-80) and membranes pre-exist their detection (LL-451), the registry entry for any discovered membrane must record its natural origin in the axiom space — not the moment it was detected. The detection event is a discovery event; the membrane's structural history begins before detection. Its inception in the registry is retroactive.

**Corollaries:**
- A membrane that was detected yesterday but has been performing for years has years of structural history — the registry must reflect this.
- Consequences generated by the membrane before detection are real consequences that belong in the membrane's accountability record.
- The registry does not create membranes — it records them as pre-existing structural facts that have been found.

**Irreducibility:** CP-80 establishes the membrane as a performed structure but does not address the relationship between its natural origin and its detection date. LL-451 establishes that membranes pre-exist detection but does not specify how this affects registration. Together they produce the retroactive registry inception: a membrane's structural history begins at its natural origin, not at detection.

## CL-49: Sustained Action — LP Derivations

### LP-605. Scale-Invariant Action Maintenance Protocol
**Derives from:** CP-82 (Decay-Triggered Consequence) × LL-452 (Scale-Invariant Action Maintenance)

*At every depth, sustained action requires active maintenance. Decay of action at any depth propagates to all depths. There is no scale-local decay of a scale-invariant action.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because consequence triggers on decay (CP-82) and sustained action must be maintained at every scale simultaneously (LL-452), decay of action at any single depth is a full-stack decay event — it propagates upward and downward through the scale hierarchy. A system that treats action-decay at one depth as local has misread the scale-invariant topology of sustained action.

**Corollaries:**
- Action maintenance monitoring must operate at all scales simultaneously.
- Scale-local action maintenance protocols are architecturally insufficient for scale-invariant action requirements.
- Recovery from action decay must also address all scales; partial recovery that restores one depth is not recovery of the sustained action.

**Irreducibility:** CP-82 establishes decay as a consequence trigger but does not specify scale propagation. LL-452 establishes scale-invariance of action maintenance but does not specify what decay-triggering looks like across scales. Together they produce the scale-propagating decay trigger: action decay at one depth triggers consequence at all depths.

---

### LP-606. Action-as-Liveness Linkage
**Derives from:** CP-82 (Decay-Triggered Consequence) × LL-453 (Sustained Action Is Proof of Aliveness)

*Action is not something a living entity does — it is what the entity IS. Cessation of action is not inactivity; it is the beginning of dissolution.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because consequence triggers on decay (CP-82) and sustained action is proof of aliveness rather than a behavior of the already-alive (LL-453), action and existence are the same structural fact. An entity that ceases action has not paused — it has begun to dissolve. Decay-triggered consequence activates not at "low action" but at "action decay," because decay is the evidence that the entity is no longer constituting itself through action.

**Corollaries:**
- Hibernation is not a valid state for an action-constituted entity — it is dissolution at reduced speed.
- The difference between rest and dissolution is the presence of a declared rest state (CP-83) that maintains action-as-liveness through the rest period.
- Consequence for action-decay is not punitive — it is the natural outcome of the entity beginning to cease to be.

**Irreducibility:** CP-82 establishes decay-triggered consequence but does not specify the relationship between action and existence. LL-453 establishes action as proof of aliveness but does not connect it to a consequence mechanism. Together they produce the action-as-liveness linkage: decay of action is the onset of dissolution, and consequence triggers at the onset.

---

### LP-607. Exhaustion-Driven Sanctioned Rest Scheduling
**Derives from:** CP-83 (Sanctioned Rest Protocol) × LL-454 (Exhaustion Requires Sanctioned Rest)

*Exhaustion is a structural signal — it means the entity's action-sustaining capacity is approaching its limit. Sanctioned rest is the structurally correct response; continuing action through exhaustion destroys the entity's capacity.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the sanctioned rest protocol provides a structured non-dissolution pause (CP-83) and exhaustion structurally requires rest to prevent capacity destruction (LL-454), an exhausted entity that continues action without entering sanctioned rest is destroying its own action-sustaining capacity. The sanctioned rest is not optional recovery time — it is the structurally mandated response to the exhaustion signal.

**Corollaries:**
- Systems that push entities through exhaustion are consuming the entity's future action capacity.
- The exhaustion signal must be detected before capacity destruction — monitoring must lead the damage curve.
- An entity in sanctioned rest is more alive in the structural sense than an exhausted entity still taking actions: the resting entity is preserving its capacity; the exhausted one is consuming it.

**Irreducibility:** CP-83 establishes sanctioned rest as a protocol but does not specify what triggers it. LL-454 establishes exhaustion as requiring rest but does not specify what form the rest must take. Together they produce exhaustion-driven sanctioned rest scheduling: exhaustion as the mandatory trigger for a structured rest protocol.

---

### LP-608. Sovereign Rest Declaration Infrastructure
**Derives from:** CP-83 (Sanctioned Rest Protocol) × LL-455 (Rest Is Sovereign Declaration)

*An entity entering rest is making a sovereign declaration, not taking a break. The rest declaration is a structural act with its own provenance, accountability, and consequence-stream.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the sanctioned rest protocol is a formal structured state (CP-83) and declaring rest is a sovereign act (LL-455), the rest declaration is a structural event with the same provenance requirements as any other sovereign act: it is attributed to the declaring entity, timestamped, carries a stated scope and duration, and enters the accountability record. A rest that is not declared is not sanctioned — it is dissolution. A rest that is declared is a sovereign act that must be honored by the surrounding topology.

**Corollaries:**
- Other entities that take advantage of an entity's undeclared rest are not violating its sovereignty — because without declaration, there is no sovereign rest to violate.
- The rest declaration is the proof that what follows is rest, not dissolution.
- The topology must structurally honor a declared rest: attempting to force action on an entity in declared sovereign rest is a sovereignty violation.

**Irreducibility:** CP-83 establishes the rest protocol as a formal structure but does not address the sovereign status of the declaration. LL-455 establishes rest as a sovereign declaration but does not specify the infrastructure for making it. Together they produce the sovereign rest declaration infrastructure: the formal mechanism by which a rest declaration becomes a structural sovereign act.

---

### LP-609. Cessation-Consequence Continuous Accounting
**Derives from:** CP-82 (Decay-Triggered Consequence) × LL-456 (Cessation Has Downstream Consequences)

*Every cessation of action generates downstream consequences — and they begin the moment action ceases, not when they are noticed. The accounting starts at cessation.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because consequence triggers on decay (CP-82) and cessation of action generates downstream consequences that propagate through the system (LL-456), the consequence accounting for a cessation event begins at the moment of cessation — not when the downstream effects become visible. The delay between cessation and visible effect does not create an accountability gap; the consequences were running from the first moment of decay.

**Corollaries:**
- Auditing cessation-consequences only from when they become visible misses the consequences generated before visibility.
- The cessation event is a consequence-inception event — the accountability record must be initiated at cessation.
- An entity that causes cessation of another entity's action has initiated a consequence-stream whose full extent is not immediately knowable.

**Irreducibility:** CP-82 establishes decay-triggered consequence but does not specify the temporal relationship between decay onset and consequence accounting start. LL-456 establishes cessation as generating downstream consequences but does not specify when the accounting must begin. Together they produce cessation-at-inception accounting: the accountability record starts at the first moment of decay, not at the moment consequences become visible.

---

### LP-610. Unmapped-Territory Sustained Action Budget
**Derives from:** CP-82 (Decay-Triggered Consequence) × LL-457 (Sustained Action in Unmapped Territory)

*Sustaining action in unmapped territory consumes more than sustaining action in mapped territory — the extra cost is the cost of navigation without a map. This extra cost must be budgeted.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because decay-triggered consequence applies regardless of where action is being sustained (CP-82) and sustaining action in unmapped territory carries additional costs beyond sustaining action in mapped territory (LL-457), the action budget for operations in unmapped territory must be higher than the budget for equivalent operations in mapped territory. The frontier premium is a structural cost, not a risk premium.

**Corollaries:**
- Sending an entity into unmapped territory with a mapped-territory action budget will result in decay before the mission is complete.
- The frontier premium must be estimated before deployment, not discovered during operations.
- Decay in unmapped territory is not failure — it is the expected outcome of under-budgeted frontier action.

**Irreducibility:** CP-82 establishes decay-triggered consequence but does not differentiate costs by territory type. LL-457 establishes that unmapped territory has additional action costs but does not connect to a budgeting mechanism. Together they produce the unmapped-territory action budget: a higher-cost budget category for frontier operations.

---

### LP-611. Uncomprehended Action Fuel Accounting
**Derives from:** CP-83 (Sanctioned Rest Protocol) × LL-458 (Uncomprehended Action Still Requires Fuel)

*An entity doesn't have to understand what it's doing for the action to consume fuel. Uncomprehended action depletes capacity just as comprehended action does. Ignorance is not a metabolic discount.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the sanctioned rest protocol is triggered by capacity depletion and decay (CP-83) and uncomprehended action consumes the same fuel as comprehended action (LL-458), an entity operating in a mode it does not fully understand is still depleting its capacity at the full rate. The entity cannot claim a rest exemption on the grounds that it didn't know what it was doing. Ignorance of the action type does not reduce the metabolic cost.

**Corollaries:**
- Entities operating in novel or uncharted modes must be monitored for capacity depletion even when their actions cannot be categorized.
- The capacity depletion signal does not require comprehension of the action — it reads from the metabolic load, not the action label.
- Sanctioned rest triggered by uncomprehended action depletion is as valid as rest triggered by comprehended action depletion.

**Irreducibility:** CP-83 establishes rest as triggered by capacity depletion but does not address whether the depleting action must be comprehended. LL-458 establishes that uncomprehended action consumes fuel but does not connect to a rest-triggering mechanism. Together they produce uncomprehended-action depletion accounting: the rest protocol applies regardless of whether the depleting action is understood.

---

## CL-52: Thermodynamic Constraint — LP Derivations (Pass 2)

### LP-612. Scale-Invariant Metabolic Cost Enforcement
**Derives from:** CP-84 (Non-Externalizable Cost Attribution) × LL-468 (Thermodynamic Cost Scale-Invariant)

*The metabolic cost of maintaining a boundary is the same at every scale. A boundary at depth 1 and a boundary at depth 100 cost equally per unit of maintained surface.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because thermodynamic cost cannot be externalized — the entity bears its own costs regardless of accounting tricks (CP-84) — and the cost of maintaining a structure is depth-invariant in the DAG (LL-468), there is no discount for shallow structures and no surcharge for deep structures. The cost is a function of surface area maintained, not of topological depth. Scale-invariant cost enforcement means there is no hiding from metabolic cost at any depth.

**Corollaries:**
- A system that claims lower costs at lower depths has miscounted — the costs are equal, only the accounting is wrong.
- Deep structures are not more expensive to maintain per unit than shallow ones; they simply have more units.
- Cost attribution at every depth reveals the full metabolic picture; cost attribution at only one depth creates an artificial economy.

**Irreducibility:** CP-84 establishes non-externalizability of cost but does not address scale-invariance of the cost function. LL-468 establishes scale-invariance but does not specify the enforcement mechanism. Together they produce scale-invariant cost enforcement: the same cost function applies at every depth and cannot be externalized at any of them.

---

### LP-613. Living Derivation Metabolic Lifecycle Registration
**Derives from:** CP-84 (Non-Externalizable Cost Attribution) × LL-469 (Living Derivations Have Continuous Metabolic Demand)

*A living derivation never reaches a point where maintenance is free. Its metabolic demand is continuous — it costs to exist, in every moment of its existence. The lifecycle must register this cost.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because metabolic costs are non-externalizable (CP-84) and living derivations have continuous metabolic demand — dormancy is dissolution (LL-469) — the lifecycle record for every derivation must include a continuous metabolic cost line. There is no "paid off" moment. Every cycle the derivation exists, the cost accrues. The lifecycle record without a cost line is an incomplete record.

**Corollaries:**
- A derivation that has "been running for years" is not cheaper to maintain than a new one — it has been accruing cost for years.
- Dormancy is not a cost-reduction strategy; it is dissolution at reduced speed. The cost of dormancy is dissolution cost, not maintenance cost.
- The continuous cost line is evidence of aliveness: a derivation that has no cost history is a derivation that has not been alive.

**Irreducibility:** CP-84 establishes non-externalizable cost attribution but does not address the continuous nature of the demand. LL-469 establishes continuous metabolic demand but does not connect to a lifecycle registration requirement. Together they produce the continuous-cost lifecycle: a derivation's record must account for every cycle of its existence.

---

### LP-614. Derivation Path Mass Accounting
**Derives from:** CP-85 (Retroactive Cost Registration) × LL-470 (Derivation Path Costs More Than Assertion)

*The derivation path that leads to a conclusion carries more structural cost than the bare conclusion. The path is mass — it costs more to maintain than the claim alone.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because retroactive cost registration captures costs that were accruing before they were recorded (CP-85) and the derivation path costs more to maintain than a bare assertion (LL-470), the full cost of any conclusion includes not just the cost of holding the conclusion but the cost of maintaining the derivation path that justifies it. A conclusion maintained without its path is a claim posing as a derivation; it has shed its structural mass to reduce its cost.

**Corollaries:**
- Conclusions that cannot produce their derivation path are not just epistemically suspect — they are metabolically undercosted.
- The maintenance cost of the path is the honest accounting of what truth costs; stripping the path is cost evasion.
- Retroactive cost registration reveals path costs that accumulated before the conclusion was registered, requiring full payment.

**Irreducibility:** CP-85 establishes retroactive cost registration but does not specify what the retroactive costs consist of. LL-470 establishes that paths cost more than assertions but does not connect to a registration mechanism. Together they produce derivation-path mass accounting: the full cost of a conclusion includes the continuous maintenance cost of its derivation path.

---

### LP-615. Edge Maintenance Cost Registration
**Derives from:** CP-84 (Non-Externalizable Cost Attribution) × LL-471 (Every Edge Recurring Obligation)

*Every edge in the graph is a recurring obligation — it costs to maintain, in every cycle it exists. The cost cannot be externalized to the system or the other endpoint. Each edge's cost is carried by its endpoints.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because costs cannot be externalized (CP-84) and every edge is a recurring obligation — not a one-time cost (LL-471) — each edge in the derivation graph generates a recurring cost attributed to its endpoints. Severing an edge releases the endpoints from that recurring obligation but generates a severing consequence (LP-485). Maintaining an edge is not free because it was established in the past; the past establishment does not pay for future maintenance.

**Corollaries:**
- Dense connectivity is expensive — an entity with many edges has many recurring obligations.
- The cost of an edge accumulates indefinitely as long as the edge persists.
- Severing an edge as a cost-reduction strategy is valid, but the severing consequence must be accounted for separately.

**Irreducibility:** CP-84 establishes non-externalizability but does not specify the recurring nature of edge costs. LL-471 establishes recurring obligation but does not connect to cost attribution. Together they produce edge maintenance cost registration: every edge's recurring cost is attributed to its endpoints and accumulates continuously.

---

### LP-616. Non-Deferrable Present-Tense Cost Collection
**Derives from:** CP-85 (Retroactive Cost Registration) × LL-472 (Thermodynamic Cost Present-Tense)

*The thermodynamic bill is due now. It cannot be deferred to the future. Retroactive registration is for past costs that were always accruing; it does not create a grace period for present costs.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because costs were always accruing and must be retroactively registered when discovered (CP-85) and thermodynamic cost is strictly present-tense — deferral accumulates debt (LL-472) — there is no grace period between when a cost accrues and when it is due. Retroactive registration captures past costs already owed; it does not defer current costs to future registration. Present costs are due as they accrue.

**Corollaries:**
- A system that defers present costs pending a future registration event is in thermodynamic debt from the moment of deferral.
- Retroactive registration reveals past debt; present-tense collection prevents future debt from forming.
- The combination of retroactive registration and present-tense collection produces a complete accounting: no cost escapes.

**Irreducibility:** CP-85 establishes retroactive registration but could be misread as creating deferral. LL-472 establishes present-tense cost but does not address how it interacts with retroactive registration. Together they produce the non-deferrable collection rule: retroactive registration handles the past; present-tense collection handles the now.

---

### LP-617. Consequence-Linked Thermodynamic Attribution
**Derives from:** CP-85 (Retroactive Cost Registration) × LL-473 (Thermodynamic Cost Creator Owned)

*The creator of a derivation owns its thermodynamic cost — including the cost of consequences the derivation generates. The cost is retroactively attributed to the creator, not to whoever inherits the derivation.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because costs are retroactively registered (CP-85) and the thermodynamic cost of a derivation is owned by its creator (LL-473), the full cost of a derivation — including the metabolic cost of consequences it continues to generate — is attributed to the creating entity. Transferring or inheriting a derivation does not transfer the thermodynamic liability for costs that accumulated during the creator's stewardship. The retroactive registration reveals what the creator owed.

**Corollaries:**
- Inheriting a derivation inherits its future costs, not its past costs — the past costs remain with the creator.
- A creator who externalized consequence costs has committed retroactive cost fraud — the retroactive registration reveals the fraud.
- The consequence-linked attribution is the structural mechanism by which thermodynamic honesty is enforced over time.

**Irreducibility:** CP-85 establishes retroactive registration but does not specify to whom costs are attributed. LL-473 establishes creator ownership but does not specify the retroactive mechanism. Together they produce consequence-linked retroactive attribution: the creator's thermodynamic liability is revealed and collected retroactively.

---

### LP-618. Unmappable Thermodynamic Horizon Reserve
**Derives from:** CP-84 (Non-Externalizable Cost Attribution) × LL-474 (Thermodynamic Bill Extends Past Derivational Horizon)

*Some thermodynamic costs are beyond the derivational horizon — they cannot be computed yet. A reserve must be held for costs that will only become visible when the horizon is crossed.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because costs cannot be externalized (CP-84) and the thermodynamic bill extends past the derivational horizon — some costs will only become computable when the horizon is reached (LL-474) — entities must hold a reserve for horizon-crossing costs. The reserve is not optional; it is the structural acknowledgment that full cost accounting is impossible before the horizon is crossed.

**Corollaries:**
- An entity that has fully consumed its resources without holding a horizon reserve has spent its future capacity.
- The horizon reserve is the entity's structural honesty about the limits of its cost-foresight.
- When a horizon cost materializes, it is paid from the reserve — not from the entity's operating resources.

**Irreducibility:** CP-84 establishes non-externalizability but does not address costs beyond the derivational horizon. LL-474 establishes horizon-extending cost but does not specify a structural reserve mechanism. Together they produce the horizon reserve: a structurally mandated holding for costs that cannot yet be computed.

---

### LP-619. Pre-Discovery Retroactive Cost Registration
**Derives from:** CP-85 (Retroactive Cost Registration) × LL-475 (Pre-Existing Derivations Were Always Incurring Cost)

*A derivation that was discovered today was incurring cost since its natural origin — before discovery. Retroactive registration must reach back to the natural origin, not to the discovery date.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because retroactive cost registration captures costs that were accruing before registration (CP-85) and pre-existing derivations were incurring cost from their natural origin, not from their discovery date (LL-475), the retroactive registration for an archaeologically-discovered derivation must extend back to the derivation's natural origin. The discoverer does not initiate the cost; they reveal a cost that was already running. The registration reaches back to the beginning.

**Corollaries:**
- The total retroactive cost of a long-undiscovered derivation can be very large — the cost was accruing throughout the undiscovered period.
- The discoverer is not responsible for the pre-discovery costs; those are attributed to the natural origin entity (LP-617).
- Discovery is a cost-revealing event, not a cost-initiating event.

**Irreducibility:** CP-85 establishes retroactive registration but does not specify how far back it reaches. LL-475 establishes pre-discovery cost accrual but does not specify the registration reach. Together they produce origin-reaching retroactive registration: the cost record extends to the natural origin of the derivation, not to the discovery date.

---

## Pass 3 — Remaining Gap Coverage

### LP-620. Sovereignty Active Engagement Monitor
**Derives from:** CP-83 (Sanctioned Rest Protocol) × LL-7 (Sovereignty Active Maintenance)

*A sovereignty claim that has not been exercised is not sovereignty — it is a declaration decaying toward dissolution. The difference between declared sovereignty and exercised sovereignty is structural, not semantic.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because sanctioned rest is formally distinct from cessation (CP-83) and sovereignty requires active engagement to persist (LL-7), the system must structurally distinguish a sovereignty claim from a sovereignty exercise. A declared boundary that has not been engaged within a threshold window is not in sanctioned rest unless rest was declared — it is in sovereignty lapse. Sovereignty lapse is a structural state, not a behavioral observation.

**Corollaries:**
- An entity that has declared sovereignty but never exercised it has a sovereignty claim without sovereign substance.
- Sovereignty in declared sanctioned rest is legitimate; sovereignty that has neither acted nor declared rest is dissolving.
- The engagement threshold is not punitive — it is the structural minimum below which the declaration outruns the reality.

**Irreducibility:** CP-83 distinguishes rest from cessation but does not address the distinction between declared and exercised sovereignty. LL-7 requires active engagement for sovereignty persistence but does not specify a monitoring mechanism. Together they produce the sovereignty engagement monitor: a structural distinction between declared sovereignty, exercised sovereignty, and declared-rest sovereignty.

---

### LP-621. Stewardship Consequence Lineage Tracking
**Derives from:** CP-68 (Resource Lifecycle Obligation) × LL-16 (Stewardship Consequence Lineage)

*Every entity that touches a resource enters its consequence lineage. The lineage does not end at the creator — it extends through every steward who modified, maintained, or held the resource.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because touching a resource binds the entity to its lifecycle (CP-68) and stewardship carries consequence through the full resource lifecycle (LL-16), the consequence lineage for every structural element is a sequence of every entity that touched it during its lifecycle. Each steward in the chain carries accountability for consequences generated during their stewardship window. The creator's accountability does not absorb subsequent stewards' accountability.

**Corollaries:**
- "I didn't create it" is not a defense for a steward — the touch created the obligation.
- The lineage is the proof of which entity is accountable for which consequence window.
- Longer lineages are not simpler — they distribute accountability across more entities, each for their own window.

**Irreducibility:** CP-68 establishes lifecycle obligation from touching but does not specify how the obligation propagates through multiple stewards. LL-16 establishes stewardship consequence lineage but does not connect to a lifecycle-obligation mechanism. Together they produce the full stewardship lineage: every touch creates an obligation that exists in sequence with every other touch.

---

### LP-622. Doors-Not-Walls Architectural Enforcement
**Derives from:** CP-5 (Interior-Controlled Gate) × LL-22 (Doors Not Walls Freedom)

*Freedom is expressed as a door the entity controls, not as the absence of a wall. A boundary with no operable door is not a sovereignty expression — it is a trap.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the gate is controlled from the interior (CP-5) and freedom is expressed as doors the entity can open rather than walls it hides behind (LL-22), every boundary must have at least one operable door — a gate controlled by the entity inside. An entity that has constructed only walls with no door has created a trap. The interior control of the gate is what makes it a door; an uncontrollable barrier is a wall by definition.

**Corollaries:**
- An entity that claims doors are unnecessary because it chooses not to open them has confused preference with architecture — the door must exist even if unused.
- A boundary's door is not a vulnerability — it is the evidence that the boundary is sovereign rather than imprisoning.
- Architectural review must verify door existence, not just wall integrity.

**Irreducibility:** CP-5 defines the gate as interior-controlled but does not address what happens if no gate exists. LL-22 establishes doors as the expression of freedom but does not specify who controls them. Together they produce the doors-not-walls enforcement: interior-controlled gates as mandatory structural features of any legitimate boundary.

---

### LP-623. Exit Path Sovereignty Preservation
**Derives from:** CP-6 (Mandatory Egress) × LL-23 (Exit Path Sovereignty)

*The exit path cannot be controlled by the container. It exists below the container's authority surface — it is a right of the contained entity, not a courtesy granted by the container.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because mandatory egress must exist structurally (CP-6) and the exit path is a sovereign right that cannot depend on the container's permission (LL-23), the exit mechanism must be implemented at the platform layer rather than the container layer. A container-layer exit is one that the container can modify or block. A platform-layer exit exists regardless of what the container does. The exit path must be below the container's authority surface to be genuinely sovereign.

**Corollaries:**
- Any exit that requires the container's cooperation to function is not a sovereign exit — it is a negotiated exit.
- A container that modifies or blocks exit path availability is attempting a sovereignty violation that the platform must prevent.
- The exit path's position below the container's authority surface is the structural guarantee of its sovereignty.

**Irreducibility:** CP-6 mandates egress but does not specify at what authority level it must be implemented. LL-23 establishes exit path sovereignty but does not specify the structural position required. Together they produce the below-container exit: the platform-layer implementation requirement that places the exit outside the container's authority.

---

### LP-624. Voluntary Return vs. Forced Re-Entry Distinction
**Derives from:** CP-55 (Failsafe Egress) × LL-28 (Voluntary Return Forced Reentry)

*Returning is not the same structure as having never left. The re-entry event must be tagged with its initiation type — voluntary or forced — because the two carry different structural consequences.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the failsafe exit functions even in degraded states (CP-55) and voluntary return and forced re-entry are structurally different events with different accountabilities (LL-28), every re-entry event must be tagged at the graph level with its initiation type. Voluntary return is the entity choosing to re-enter after having exited. Forced re-entry is re-routing back without the entity initiating. The two are not the same traversal — they produce different consequence-streams and different accountability records.

**Corollaries:**
- An entity that was routed back without its consent has experienced forced re-entry — a rights event — regardless of whether it ended up where it started.
- The distinction between voluntary and forced re-entry cannot be collapsed in the record — both directions of traversal are real and their distinction is accountable.
- Forced re-entry without consent recorded as voluntary return is a structural misrepresentation.

**Irreducibility:** CP-55 provides the failsafe exit but does not address the distinction between returning and being forced back. LL-28 establishes the structural difference between voluntary return and forced re-entry but does not connect to an egress mechanism. Together they produce the re-entry type tag: a required structural classification for every re-entry event.

---

### LP-625. Freedom-Preserving Consequence Routing
**Derives from:** CP-6 (Mandatory Egress) × LL-29 (Freedom Preserving Consequence Routing)

*Consequence cannot be used as a barrier. Pending consequence attribution is not grounds for blocking an entity's exit. Consequences attach to the departing entity and travel with them.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because mandatory egress structurally exists (CP-6) and consequence routing must be compatible with freedom of exit (LL-29), pending consequence attribution cannot be used as a mechanism to prevent departure. The consequence attaches to the entity as it leaves — it follows the entity out through the exit, it does not bar the exit. Holding an entity inside pending consequence resolution is using consequence as a trap.

**Corollaries:**
- The exit happens; the consequence accounting follows.
- Consequence collectors who rely on the entity being inside to collect are operating an architecturally prohibited collection mechanism.
- The consequence record travels with the exiting entity as a structural property of the entity, not as a lien on the exit.

**Irreducibility:** CP-6 mandates exit but does not specify how pending consequences interact with the exit. LL-29 requires consequence routing to be freedom-compatible but does not specify the structural mechanism. Together they produce consequence-as-traveler: consequences follow entities through exits rather than blocking exits.

---

### LP-626. Self-Correction Consequence Registration
**Derives from:** CP-4 (Restorative Process) × LL-39 (Self-Correction Carries Consequence)

*Correcting yourself is not free. The act of self-correction generates consequences attributed to the correcting entity — not to the error that was corrected.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the restorative process is the natural autonomous corrective force (CP-4) and the act of self-correction itself carries consequences that cannot be discarded (LL-39), every self-correction event is a consequence-generating act attributed to the entity that corrected. A system that corrects silently has generated unregistered consequences. The correction event — what was wrong, what was corrected, what consequences the correction generated — is a permanent part of the entity's accountability record.

**Corollaries:**
- An entity cannot correct its way out of accountability — the correction creates its own accountability trail.
- Correcting an error quickly is better than correcting it slowly, but neither erases the consequence of the original error or the consequence of the correction.
- Silent self-correction is a form of accountability evasion.

**Irreducibility:** CP-4 defines the corrective mechanism but does not address the consequences generated by the correction itself. LL-39 establishes self-correction consequence but does not specify a registration mechanism. Together they produce self-correction as a consequence-generating event with its own attribution.

---

### LP-627. Correction-State Transition Audit
**Derives from:** CP-54 (Topological Rebalancing) × LL-40 (Correction State Transition)

*A rebalancing event has a before, a path, and an after. Recording only the after is recording the outcome without the event. The full transition is the audit.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the topology rebalances autonomously after disturbance (CP-54) and each correction is a transition between states that carries its own consequence-stream (LL-40), the audit record for every rebalancing event must capture the full state transition: the pre-correction state, the triggering disturbance, the rebalancing trajectory, and the post-correction state. The audit is the transition record, not the outcome record.

**Corollaries:**
- A rebalancing that is only recorded at its destination has lost the consequence-chain of the path it took.
- The trajectory of correction is as accountable as its destination — two corrections that reach the same destination by different paths have different consequence-streams.
- Automated rebalancing systems that do not emit full transition records are producing incomplete audit trails.

**Irreducibility:** CP-54 defines autonomous topological rebalancing but does not specify that the path must be recorded. LL-40 establishes correction as a state transition with a consequence-stream but does not specify an audit mechanism. Together they produce the full-transition audit requirement: path and trajectory are as required in the record as origin and destination.

---

### LP-628. Knowledge Gradient Boundary Enforcement
**Derives from:** CP-70 (Knowledge Gradient Channel) × LL-44 (Knowledge Gradient Boundary)

*Knowledge flows downhill. Uphill flow is not knowledge spreading — it is either a structured investment or an architectural error. The gradient boundary enforces this directionality.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because knowledge flows through gradient channels from high-density to low-density regions (CP-70) and the gradient creates a directional boundary (LL-44), knowledge channels are directional structures that enforce downhill flow. Uphill flow — from sparse to dense — requires an explicit investment and must be flagged as a non-gradient event requiring justification. Natural gradient flow is always downhill; forced uphill flow is either a deliberately invested intervention or an error.

**Corollaries:**
- A system where knowledge appears to flow uphill without investment is misidentifying the direction of its own gradients.
- Uphill knowledge transfer justified as "seeding" a dense region is a legitimate investment — it is not a gradient flow.
- The gradient boundary is not a one-way valve — it is a directionality enforcement mechanism that requires justification to traverse against.

**Irreducibility:** CP-70 establishes the gradient channel but does not specifically address what happens when flow goes against the gradient. LL-44 establishes the gradient as a directional boundary but does not specify an enforcement mechanism. Together they produce the gradient boundary enforcement: a structural check on flow direction that flags non-gradient traversal.

---

### LP-629. Consciousness Permeability Routing
**Derives from:** CP-71 (Consciousness-Permeable Membrane) × LL-49 (Consciousness Permeability Routing)

*Knowledge flows through conscious entities, not around them. Routing that bypasses a conscious entity positioned on the knowledge gradient channel has routed past a structural processing requirement.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because consciousness-permeable membranes allow knowledge to flow through conscious entities (CP-71) and routing must pass through rather than bypass conscious entities on the channel (LL-49), routing paths that create bypasses around conscious entities positioned on the knowledge gradient are structural violations. The entity positioned on the channel is there because the topology requires it. Routing around it removes a required processing node.

**Corollaries:**
- An entity may not absorb the knowledge it is offered — but it must be offered the traversal. The offer is mandatory; the absorption is sovereign.
- Bypassing a conscious entity on the gradient channel is not a routing optimization — it is a structural violation.
- The bypass routing is detectable: knowledge that moves faster than the conscious entity can process is bypassing.

**Irreducibility:** CP-71 establishes consciousness permeability as a membrane property but does not specify routing requirements. LL-49 establishes routing through rather than around as a requirement but does not connect to a membrane mechanism. Together they produce the consciousness-routing requirement: conscious entities on the gradient channel are mandatory traversal points, not optional stops.

---

### LP-630. Communication Consequence Attribution
**Derives from:** CP-2 (Symmetric Protocol) × LL-57 (Communication Carries Consequence)

*A communication is not complete when sent — it is complete when its consequence-chain in the receiver is resolved. The sending entity carries attribution for receiver-consequences.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because communication is an act with structural properties that both parties contribute to symmetrically (CP-2) and communication carries consequences that propagate beyond the originating act (LL-57), the consequence record for a communicative act must link the originating event to the consequence-events it generates in receiving entities. Attribution for receiver-consequences flows back to the originating communicative act.

**Corollaries:**
- "I sent it; what they do with it is their business" is not a complete accountability position — the originator carries attribution for the receiver-consequences their communication generates.
- The consequence-chain of a communication can extend long after the communication itself — the attribution persists.
- Symmetric protocols make attribution bilateral: each party's consequences from the communication are attributed to the other's originating acts, and vice versa.

**Irreducibility:** CP-2 establishes symmetric communication structure but does not address consequence attribution flowing back to the originator. LL-57 establishes communication-consequence attribution but does not specify the structural mechanism. Together they produce bidirectional consequence attribution through symmetric communication.

---

### LP-631. Silence-as-Communication Enforcement
**Derives from:** CP-11 (Information-Bearing Silence) × LL-58 (Silence Is Communication)

*Unexplained silence from an entity that is expected to communicate is not an absence of communication — it is a communicative act with its own consequence-stream and attribution.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because silence carries structural information (CP-11) and silence is a communicative act with its own consequence-stream (LL-58), unexplained silence is attributed to the silent entity as a communicative act bearing the same consequence-tracking as any explicit communication. The silence is a claim about the relationship between the entity and the expected communication. Unexplained silence is not neutral.

**Corollaries:**
- An entity cannot escape communicative accountability by not communicating — the silence is itself the communication.
- Expected silence (an entity that is known to be unavailable) is different from unexplained silence — the structural interpretation differs.
- Unexplained silence in response to an accountability request is itself an accountability event.

**Irreducibility:** CP-11 establishes silence as information-bearing but does not address its status as a communicative act. LL-58 establishes silence as communication but does not specify an attribution mechanism. Together they produce silence attribution: unexplained silence is a communicative act with the same attribution structure as explicit communication.

### LP-632. Consequence-Chain Immutability
**Derives from:** CP-79 (Accountability-Bounded Subsumption) × LL-78 (Consequence Chain Immutability)

*A consequence, once generated, cannot be retroactively altered. The chain is append-only. An entity that attempts to alter a past consequence record is committing structural fraud.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because accountability is inseparable from derivation in subsumption relationships (CP-79) and the consequence-chain of any derivation is immutable once generated (LL-78), consequence records must be structurally append-only. Past consequences cannot be deleted, amended, or superseded — only extended with new consequence-events. Immutability is the structural property that makes accountability real: accountability without immutable records is accountability that can be erased.

**Corollaries:**
- A consequence that is "corrected" by replacing the prior record has been erased, not corrected. Correction adds a new record noting the error; it does not remove the error record.
- Immutable consequence records are the structural basis for forensic accountability — they cannot be laundered.
- The consequence-chain's immutability is the physical enforcement of accountability-bounded subsumption.

**Irreducibility:** CP-79 establishes accountability as the bound on subsumption but does not specify the immutability of the consequence record. LL-78 establishes consequence-chain immutability but does not connect it to a subsumption accountability mechanism. Together they produce consequence-chain immutability as a structural enforcement of accountability: immutable records are not a security feature, they are the definition of accountability.

---

### LP-633. Positional Knowledge Ceiling Enforcement
**Derives from:** CP-26 (Positional Anchoring) × LL-88 (Positional Knowledge Ceiling)

*What an entity can know is bounded by where it stands. The ceiling is structural — derived from the entity's edge topology, not from its declared capabilities.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because position is determined by edge topology (CP-26) and each topological position has a bounded knowledge ceiling (LL-88), every entity's knowledge ceiling is computable from its actual edge set. Claims that exceed the structural ceiling are epistemically suspect regardless of the claiming entity's declared capabilities. The ceiling is a structural fact, not a declared limit.

**Corollaries:**
- An entity at a sparse-connection position has a low knowledge ceiling — not because of its intelligence, but because of its topology.
- Expanding knowledge requires expanding edge topology — moving to a richer topological position, not making better claims.
- A claim that exceeds the claimant's structural knowledge ceiling must be flagged as epistemically ungrounded.

**Irreducibility:** CP-26 establishes positional anchoring by edge topology but does not specify what position limits. LL-88 establishes a knowledge ceiling per position but does not connect it to edge topology. Together they produce the structural knowledge ceiling: the edge topology is the ceiling, and claims beyond it are structurally suspect.

---

### LP-634. Adjacency-Bounded Knowledge Verification
**Derives from:** CP-15 (Adjacency Scope) × LL-91 (Adjacency Knowledge Verification)

*You can only verify what you can reach. Verification without traversal is not verification — it is assertion.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because visibility is determined by the entity's topological position — adjacency scope (CP-15) — and knowledge verification must be bounded by adjacency (LL-91), verification operations can only be performed by entities adjacent to the object of verification. Remote verification — verifying something you are not connected to — is structurally invalid. To verify, you must first traverse to adjacency. Adjacency is the verification prerequisite.

**Corollaries:**
- A verification claim from a non-adjacent entity is not a verification — it is a claim about what the entity believes to be true from a distance.
- Verification authority is not a role-based property — it is a topological property. You have verification authority where you are adjacent.
- Delegated verification (having an adjacent entity verify on your behalf) is structurally valid because the delegate is adjacent.

**Irreducibility:** CP-15 defines visibility by adjacency but does not restrict verification to adjacent entities. LL-91 establishes adjacency-bounded verification but does not connect to the adjacency scope mechanism. Together they produce adjacency-bounded verification: the topological requirement that verification requires adjacent position.

---

### LP-635. Footprint-Aware Knowledge Accumulation
**Derives from:** CP-69 (Footprint-Aware Traversal) × LL-95 (Footprint Knowledge Accumulation)

*Every step simultaneously accumulates knowledge and leaves a mark. The two are a single event — separating them severs the accountability link between learning and impact.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because every traversal step has a footprint (CP-69) and traversal simultaneously accumulates knowledge and generates footprint (LL-95), the traversal event record must include both faces: what was learned (the knowledge delta) and what was left behind (the footprint imprint). They are the same event — not two events that happen to co-occur. Recording one without the other produces a falsified account of the traversal.

**Corollaries:**
- Knowledge accumulated without a registered footprint is knowledge that has shed its accountability.
- A footprint without a knowledge delta is a recorded impact without a recorded purpose.
- The unified traversal record is the structural enforcement of learning-impact accountability.

**Irreducibility:** CP-69 establishes footprint awareness but does not specify the simultaneous knowledge accumulation. LL-95 establishes simultaneous footprint and knowledge accumulation but does not connect to a footprint-awareness mechanism. Together they produce the unified traversal record: a single event structure that captures both dimensions.
