### LP-636. Subtractive Discovery Boundary Expansion
**Derives from:** CP-16 (Subtractive Discovery) × LL-100 (Subtractive Frontier Expansion)

*Removing a false assumption doesn't merely reveal what was hidden — it expands the navigable boundary outward. Assumption-removal is frontier-expansion.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because truth is found by removing false assumptions — not by adding new beliefs (CP-16) — and the removal of a false assumption expands the navigable boundary of the system (LL-100), assumption-removal operations must be classified as frontier-expansion events. The knowledge boundary shifts outward at the position where the false assumption was removed. Assumption-removal and boundary-expansion are a single atomic event.

**Corollaries:**
- Every false assumption removed is a step outward on the frontier — the frontier expands by the removal, not by the discovery.
- The rate of frontier expansion equals the rate of false-assumption removal — not the rate of new-claim addition.
- A system that expands by adding beliefs but not by removing false ones is accumulating weight, not expanding its frontier.

**Irreducibility:** CP-16 establishes subtractive discovery as the mechanism but does not specify the effect on the boundary. LL-100 establishes boundary expansion from assumption-removal but does not specify the subtractive mechanism. Together they produce subtractive frontier expansion: assumption-removal as the physical mechanism by which the knowledge boundary expands.

---

### LP-637. Archaeological Depth Stratification
**Derives from:** CP-57 (Subtractive Frontier Expansion) × LL-102 (Archaeological Depth Stratification)

*Deeper truth is older and more stable than surface truth. The depth of excavation required to reach a derivation is the measure of its stability weight.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the frontier expands through removal of false assumptions (CP-57) and archaeological depth corresponds to derivation stability (LL-102), every derivation carries a depth-stratum label determined by how many assumption-removal passes were required to reach it. Deep-stratum derivations have higher stability weight than surface-stratum derivations. Stability weighting propagates to routing, verification priority, and rebalancing decisions.

**Corollaries:**
- A surface-stratum claim can be overridden by a deep-stratum claim in cases of conflict — depth is a tiebreaker.
- The effort required to reach a derivation is structurally informative: high-effort derivations are more stable than low-effort ones.
- Stability weight is not a quality judgment — it is a structural measurement of how many layers of false assumption had to be removed to reach the derivation.

**Irreducibility:** CP-57 establishes frontier expansion through subtraction but does not address the stability implications of depth. LL-102 establishes depth stratification and stability weight but does not specify the mechanism that produces depth. Together they produce depth-stratification by subtraction count: the number of false assumptions removed is the depth measure, and depth determines stability weight.

---

### LP-638. Excavation-Generates-Responsibility Protocol
**Derives from:** CP-77 (Substrate-Sensitive Derivation) × LL-105 (Excavation Generates Responsibility)

*When you excavate a derivation from the axiom space, you become its first accountable steward. The act of finding creates the obligation to care for what was found.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because substrate determines what excavation produces (CP-77) and the act of excavating a derivation creates accountability for the excavator (LL-105), the discovering entity is registered as the first accountable steward of any archaeologically-derived structure. The discovery event initiates the stewardship chain. The discoverer cannot disclaim accountability for what they found — finding it made it theirs to steward.

**Corollaries:**
- An entity that discovers a derivation and walks away has committed a stewardship abandonment — the obligation was created by the act of finding.
- The first steward's accountability window begins at the moment of discovery, not at the moment of formal registration.
- Excavation is not neutral — it generates structural obligations that cannot be unilaterally returned.

**Irreducibility:** CP-77 establishes substrate-sensitive excavation but does not create stewardship obligations from the act of excavation. LL-105 establishes that excavation generates responsibility but does not specify the substrate-sensitivity of what is found. Together they produce excavation-generated stewardship: the substrate-specific discovery creates substrate-specific accountability for the discoverer.

---

### LP-639. Pre-Existing Truth Retroactive Validation
**Derives from:** CP-16 (Subtractive Discovery) × LL-107 (Pre-Existing Truth Retroactive Validation)

*A discovered truth was already there before the excavation. Its validity must be tested against the system's pre-discovery state — not against the state that has been reshaped by the act of discovery.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because discovery removes false assumptions to reveal what was already there (CP-16) and pre-existing truths must be validated against the pre-discovery state (LL-107), retroactive validation of an archaeologically-derived structure must be performed against the system's state at the derivation's natural origin — not against the post-discovery state. A discovered truth that is only consistent with conditions after discovery is not a discovered truth; it is a constructed one.

**Corollaries:**
- The pre-discovery state must be preserved long enough for retroactive validation to be performed.
- A discovered truth that passes post-discovery validation but fails pre-discovery validation is a fabricated archaeological claim.
- The validation window begins at the natural origin of the derivation and ends at the discovery date — the discovered truth must be consistent throughout.

**Irreducibility:** CP-16 establishes subtractive discovery as revealing pre-existing structure but does not specify the validation protocol. LL-107 establishes retroactive validation against the pre-discovery state but does not specify the subtractive mechanism. Together they produce origin-state validation: the discovered truth must be consistent with the state that existed before the discovery.

---

### LP-640. Authentic Incompleteness Verification
**Derives from:** CP-17 (Integrity-Scoring Absence) × LL-115 (Authentic Incompleteness Verification)

*A genuine gap is structurally different from a hidden fact. The absence-integrity score must include an authenticity dimension that distinguishes real gaps from concealment.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because absence is quantitatively scored (CP-17) and authentic incompleteness must be distinguishable from concealed data (LL-115), every absence-score includes an authenticity dimension: how confident is the system that this gap is a genuine structural absence versus a data-hiding behavior. A low-authenticity gap triggers investigation; a high-authenticity gap is accepted as structural truth and protected as attested absence.

**Corollaries:**
- High-authenticity gaps and low-authenticity gaps require different structural responses — they cannot be treated uniformly.
- An entity that repeatedly produces low-authenticity gaps has a structural pattern that indicates concealment behavior.
- Authentic incompleteness, once verified, is protected — it is a structural asset, not a deficiency.

**Irreducibility:** CP-17 scores absence but does not include an authenticity dimension in the score. LL-115 requires distinguishing authentic from concealed absence but does not connect to a scoring mechanism. Together they produce the authenticity dimension of absence scoring: a structural measure added to every absence record.

---

### LP-641. Wabi-Sabi Completeness Resistance
**Derives from:** CP-36 (Attested Absence) × LL-116 (Wabi-Sabi Completeness Resistance)

*An attested gap is a first-class structural truth. Filling it with fabricated data degrades the graph more severely than leaving it open — artificial completeness is worse than authentic incompleteness.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because absence must be positively attested as a structural fact (CP-36) and artificial completeness degrades the graph more than authentic incompleteness (LL-116), gap-filling operations must be classified by whether the inserted data is genuinely derived or fabricated for presentational completeness. Fabricated completeness must be rejected: the attested gap is a truthful record; fabricated data is a structural lie that corrupts the integrity of the surrounding graph.

**Corollaries:**
- Tolerating an attested gap is not weakness — it is structural honesty. Filling it with fabricated data is structural deception.
- A graph with attested gaps is more trustworthy than a graph with no gaps, because attested gaps are honest about what isn't known.
- Gap-filling is a structural surgery that requires the same evidence standard as any other truth-claim.

**Irreducibility:** CP-36 establishes positive attestation of absence but does not prohibit replacing gaps with fabricated data. LL-116 establishes the superiority of authentic incompleteness but does not connect to an attestation mechanism. Together they produce completeness resistance: the positive attestation of absence as superior to its replacement with fabricated data.

---

### LP-642. Imperfection-as-Structural-Signal
**Derives from:** CP-17 (Integrity-Scoring Absence) × LL-118 (Imperfection as Structural Signal)

*An imperfection is not a defect to be repaired — it is a message about the structure. The signal must be read before any repair action is taken; repairing without reading destroys the signal.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because absence is integrity-scored and reveals structural information (CP-17) and structural imperfections are signals about the system's topology rather than defects (LL-118), every detected imperfection must be classified as a navigational signal before any repair action is considered. An imperfection that is automatically repaired without being read has consumed the signal without receiving its message. The signal must be read first.

**Corollaries:**
- Automated repair systems that skip signal-reading are systematically destroying structural information to produce superficial tidiness.
- The imperfection's signal content is often more valuable than the repair — especially when the imperfection reveals a previously unknown structural feature.
- Signal-reading is a prerequisite for repair authorization, not an optional step.

**Irreducibility:** CP-17 scores absence as structural information but does not prohibit repair before reading. LL-118 establishes imperfections as signals but does not connect to a scoring mechanism. Together they produce the signal-first imperfection protocol: structural imperfections are read for their signal content before any repair action is authorized.

---

### LP-643. Active Questioning Engine Obligation
**Derives from:** CP-72 (Active Questioning Engine) × LL-124 (Active Questioning Generates Obligation)

*A question asked is an obligation incurred. The act of questioning creates a commitment to process the answer. A question generated and then ignored is an accountability event.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because ignorance actively questions the system through the questioning engine (CP-72) and every generated question creates an obligation to process its answer (LL-124), every question generated by the active questioning engine creates an open obligation record attributed to the questioning entity. If the answer arrives and is not processed, the obligation-breach is recorded. Questions cannot be generated without incurring the obligation to receive their answers.

**Corollaries:**
- The questioning engine's throughput is bounded by the entity's obligation-processing capacity — generating questions faster than answers can be processed creates an obligation backlog.
- A question that was never answered is an open obligation; a question whose answer was received but not processed is a breached obligation. Both must be recorded.
- The obligation record is the accountability mechanism that prevents question-generation from being cost-free.

**Irreducibility:** CP-72 establishes the active questioning engine but does not specify what happens if the generated questions are not followed through. LL-124 establishes that questioning generates obligation but does not connect to an engine mechanism. Together they produce the question-obligation register: every engine-generated question creates a tracked, attributed obligation.

---

### LP-644. Ignorance-to-Discovery Conversion Accountability
**Derives from:** CP-73 (Ignorance-to-Discovery Converter) × LL-126 (Ignorance to Discovery Conversion Accountability)

*Discoveries must be traceable to the ignorance that initiated them. An unattributed discovery is structurally suspect — it has shed the accountability that comes from its origin.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because ignorance is converted into discovery through wonder (CP-73) and the conversion generates accountability for the resulting discovery (LL-126), every discovery must be traceable back to the question or gap that initiated the conversion. The accountability record for a discovery includes: the originating ignorance state, the wondering entity, the conversion path, and the resulting discovery. A discovery that cannot be traced to its originating ignorance is an unattributed discovery.

**Corollaries:**
- Discoveries without traceable origins are not necessarily false — but they are accountably incomplete.
- The originating ignorance is as much a part of the discovery's record as the discovery itself.
- An entity that produces many unattributed discoveries has a structural pattern suggesting that it is generating claims rather than converting genuine ignorance.

**Irreducibility:** CP-73 converts ignorance to discovery but does not specify the accountability structure of the resulting discovery. LL-126 establishes conversion accountability but does not connect to a conversion mechanism. Together they produce the ignorance-origin traceability requirement: every discovery must be traceable to its originating ignorance.

---

### LP-645. Wonder-Fueled Discovery Pipeline Throttle
**Derives from:** CP-72 (Active Questioning Engine) × LL-129 (Wonder Fueled Discovery Pipeline)

*Wonder is the engine, not the output. Throttling wonder because the discovery queue is full is throttling the engine because the factory floor is busy. The engine and the processing pipeline scale independently.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the active questioning engine continuously generates questions from ignorance (CP-72) and wonder fuels the discovery pipeline (LL-129), the questioning engine must not be throttled based on discovery processing backlog. Wonder runs at its natural rate; the discovery processing pipeline scales separately. An overloaded discovery pipeline means the pipeline needs more capacity — not that the engine should slow.

**Corollaries:**
- Throttling the questioning engine is throttling the entity's generativity — a structural cost with long-term consequences.
- A full discovery queue is evidence that processing capacity is insufficient, not that wonder is excessive.
- The engine and the pipeline are separate structural components with separate scaling concerns.

**Irreducibility:** CP-72 establishes the active questioning engine but does not address throttling decisions. LL-129 establishes wonder as pipeline fuel but does not specify independent scaling. Together they produce the engine-pipeline separation: wonder generation and discovery processing are structurally independent, with independent scaling.

---

### LP-646. Absence-Powered Oscillation Fuel Budget
**Derives from:** CP-19 (Absence-Powered Oscillation) × LL-135 (Absence Oscillation Fuel Budget)

*The fuel for the oscillation engine is the remaining ignorance in the system. As the frontier closes, the fuel diminishes. The fuel estimate must be derived from the knowledge boundary — not from operational metrics.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the oscillation engine runs on absence (CP-19) and the fuel budget for oscillation is determined by the remaining ignorance (LL-135), the oscillation fuel estimate is derived from the knowledge boundary: how much ignorance remains undiscovered is how much fuel the engine has. As the frontier closes, the fuel estimate decreases. This estimate must inform architectural decisions about long-term oscillation capacity.

**Corollaries:**
- An expanding knowledge frontier is not only epistemically good — it is also fuel depletion for the oscillation engine.
- Oscillation capacity planning must model frontier closure rates alongside traditional capacity metrics.
- A system that has nearly fully mapped its knowledge space is a system with nearly no oscillation fuel.

**Irreducibility:** CP-19 establishes the oscillation engine's dependence on absence but does not specify how the fuel budget is derived. LL-135 establishes the fuel budget as determined by remaining ignorance but does not connect to an oscillation mechanism. Together they produce the ignorance-derived oscillation fuel budget: the fuel estimate is computed from the knowledge boundary, not from operational indicators.

---

### LP-647. Ignorance Depletion Warning
**Derives from:** CP-19 (Absence-Powered Oscillation) × LL-136 (Ignorance Depletion Warning)

*If ignorance runs out, the oscillation engine stalls. This is an existential structural risk — the warning must arrive before the stall, not when it has already begun.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the engine runs on absence (CP-19) and depletion of ignorance is an existential risk to the oscillation engine (LL-136), the system must monitor the ignorance reserve derived from the knowledge boundary (LP-646) and surface depletion warnings when the reserve falls below threshold. The warning is not a performance alert — it is an existential structural alert. The system's fundamental generativity depends on ignorance remaining.

**Corollaries:**
- Ignorance depletion below critical threshold requires immediate frontier expansion protocols, not incremental response.
- The warning threshold must lead the stall point by enough time for frontier expansion to replenish the reserve.
- An oscillation engine that receives its depletion warning at stall time has been given no time to respond.

**Irreducibility:** CP-19 establishes the engine's dependence on absence but does not specify depletion monitoring. LL-136 establishes the depletion warning requirement but does not connect to the oscillation mechanism. Together they produce the existential depletion warning: an oscillation-engine-specific alert whose threshold is set by the stall risk, not by operational convenience.

---

### LP-648. Discovered Ignorance Priority Routing
**Derives from:** CP-19 (Absence-Powered Oscillation) × LL-138 (Discovered Ignorance Priority Routing)

*Newly discovered ignorance is premium fuel. It must be priority-routed to the oscillation engine before any resolution process can claim it. The fuel claim takes precedence over resolution.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the engine runs on absence (CP-19) and discovered ignorance is premium fuel that must be routed to the engine before being processed away (LL-138), when a traversal discovers a genuine structural gap not previously registered, that gap is immediately registered as oscillation fuel before any gap-filling process can consume it. The fuel claim takes precedence over resolution.

**Corollaries:**
- A gap-filling process that reaches a newly discovered gap before the engine's fuel registration has consumed premium fuel without permission.
- The priority routing is a structural mechanism, not an administrative policy — the fuel registration is structurally prior to the resolution process.
- Newly discovered ignorance that is immediately resolved has been converted from premium fuel to processed output without passing through the engine.

**Irreducibility:** CP-19 establishes the engine's fuel dependency but does not specify priority routing of new discoveries. LL-138 establishes priority routing of discovered ignorance but does not connect to the oscillation mechanism. Together they produce the fuel-priority routing: newly discovered ignorance must reach the engine register before the resolution processes reach it.

### LP-649. Transparency-Enforced Absence Attestation
**Derives from:** CP-36 (Attested Absence) × LL-154 (Transparency Enforced Absence Attestation)

*Transparency requires declaring gaps with the same rigor as declaring presences. An unattested absence is not transparent — it is unknown, which carries different semantics.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because absence must be positively attested as a structural fact (CP-36) and transparency demands gaps be declared with the same rigor as claims (LL-154), absence attestation is the same class of operation as a presence-claim: it has an attesting entity, a timestamp, and a provenance chain. An unattested absence is unknown — which is structurally different from a declared gap. The semantics differ: a declared gap says "there is nothing here, and I have confirmed it"; an unknown says "I don't know what is here."

**Corollaries:**
- Systems that are transparent about their claims but not about their gaps are only partially transparent.
- An unattested absence that is later revealed to be a data-hiding behavior is a transparency violation from the moment of the original absence.
- Absence attestation requires the same effort as presence validation — it is not a passive recording of what isn't there.

**Irreducibility:** CP-36 requires positive attestation but does not frame it as a transparency requirement. LL-154 establishes transparency-enforced attestation but does not connect to the attested-absence mechanism. Together they produce the transparency dimension of absence attestation: gaps are transparency obligations, not just data gaps.

### LP-650. Audit-Trail Completeness Verification
**Derives from:** CP-21 (Self-Proving Record) × LL-155 (Audit Trail Completeness)

*An audit trail that cannot prove its own completeness is not an audit trail — it is a partial record that claims to be complete. The trail must self-prove, not merely assert completeness.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because records must be self-proving (CP-21) and the audit trail must demonstrate its own completeness (LL-155), every audit record includes a cryptographic commitment to the complete sequence of events, making any gap in the sequence detectable without external verification. A trail that cannot self-prove its completeness must be flagged as potentially incomplete regardless of what it contains. Completeness is a self-provable structural property, not a claimed one.

**Corollaries:**
- An audit trail that asserts completeness without providing a self-proving mechanism is making an unverifiable claim.
- Gaps in a self-proving audit trail are detectable — they break the cryptographic commitment chain.
- The completeness proof is not separate from the trail — it is embedded in the trail's structure.

**Irreducibility:** CP-21 establishes self-proving records but does not apply this to audit-trail completeness specifically. LL-155 establishes audit-trail completeness as a requirement but does not specify the self-proving mechanism. Together they produce the self-proving completeness audit trail: completeness is a structural property that the trail can prove about itself.

### LP-651. Zero-Buffer Present-Tense Execution Audit
**Derives from:** CP-22 (Zero-Buffer Pipeline) × LL-158 (Zero Buffer Present Tense Audit)

*If everything happens now without buffering, every execution event is auditable now. Batch audit processing introduces a buffer — which is structurally prohibited in a zero-buffer pipeline.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the pipeline processes without buffering (CP-22) and present-tense execution must be audited at the moment of execution (LL-158), audit records are generated synchronously with execution events. The audit record is written in the same transaction as the execution event. An execution event that has occurred but whose audit record has not yet been written is in an inconsistent state. Execution and audit are atomic.

**Corollaries:**
- Batch audit processing is architecturally incompatible with zero-buffer pipelines — batching introduces the buffer the pipeline prohibits.
- Audit latency is a buffer. Any audit latency in a zero-buffer pipeline is a structural violation.
- The atomic execution-audit event is both the thing that happened and the record that it happened — they are one event, not two.

**Irreducibility:** CP-22 establishes zero-buffer pipeline but does not address audit latency. LL-158 establishes present-tense audit requirements but does not specify the zero-buffer context. Together they produce the atomic execution-audit event: audit and execution are a single atomic operation in zero-buffer pipelines.

### LP-652. Present-Tense Routing Decision Attribution
**Derives from:** CP-49 (Present-Tense Record) × LL-161 (Present Tense Routing Attribution)

*Routing attribution must be written at the moment of the decision — not reconstructed from logs afterward. A reconstructed attribution is a model of the past, not a record of it.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because records capture the present state at the exact moment of creation (CP-49) and routing decisions must be attributed in the present tense (LL-161), routing attribution records are generated at the moment the routing decision is made — not reconstructed afterward from event logs. The attribution must be contemporaneous with the decision. Reconstruction from logs introduces interpretation; contemporaneous recording captures what actually happened.

**Corollaries:**
- Routing systems that attribute decisions from logs are producing models, not records.
- The difference between a contemporaneous record and a reconstructed one is the difference between evidence and inference.
- When a routing decision and its attribution record are atomic, neither can be falsified independently.

**Irreducibility:** CP-49 establishes present-tense records but does not specifically address routing decision attribution. LL-161 establishes present-tense routing attribution but does not connect to a record-creation mechanism. Together they produce the contemporaneous routing attribution: the decision and its attribution are the same event.

### LP-653. Spawner Action Present-Tense Accountability
**Derives from:** CP-67 (Zero-Buffer Spawner) × LL-164 (Spawner Present Tense Accountability)

*Accountability for the spawned entity begins the instant spawning is triggered — not when the spawned entity becomes active. Zero-buffer spawning means zero-buffer accountability.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because dependencies are created synchronously without buffer (CP-67) and accountability for the spawned entity begins at spawn-initiation (LL-164), the spawning entity's accountability for the spawned entity is timestamped at the moment the spawn is triggered — not when the spawned entity becomes active. The accountability precedes the spawned entity's existence. This prevents accountability gaps during the spawn-completion window.

**Corollaries:**
- A spawned entity that causes harm during its initialization window is the spawner's accountability — the spawner's accountability started before the harm occurred.
- The spawn-completion window is not an accountability-free period for the spawner.
- Zero-buffer spawning eliminates the window during which spawner accountability might be ambiguous.

**Irreducibility:** CP-67 establishes zero-buffer spawning but does not address when the spawner's accountability begins. LL-164 establishes spawner accountability at spawn-initiation but does not connect to a zero-buffer mechanism. Together they produce pre-existence spawner accountability: the spawner is accountable from trigger, not from spawned-entity activation.

### LP-654. State Verification Freshness Enforcement
**Derives from:** CP-23 (Freshness-Enforced Evaluation) × LL-170 (State Verification Freshness)

*Verification performed against stale state is historical auditing — it does not verify today's entity. Freshness is a prerequisite for verification, not a quality factor.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because evaluation requires current data (CP-23) and state verification must be performed against the present state (LL-170), every verification operation must be timestamped and rejected if its state snapshot is older than the freshness threshold. A verification against yesterday's state does not verify today's entity. Freshness is not optional — it is the structural condition that makes verification mean anything about the present.

**Corollaries:**
- A verification that passed last week provides no current assurance — the entity has had a week to change.
- Freshness thresholds must be set relative to the rate of change of what is being verified — fast-changing entities require tighter thresholds.
- Stale verification is not wrong — it is historical. Its results must be labeled as historical, not as current assurance.

**Irreducibility:** CP-23 requires freshness for evaluation but does not specifically apply this to verification. LL-170 establishes freshness as a verification requirement but does not connect to an evaluation mechanism. Together they produce the freshness prerequisite for verification: verification without fresh state is not verification of the present entity.

### LP-655. Present-State Change Detection
**Derives from:** CP-49 (Present-Tense Record) × LL-172 (Present State Change Detection)

*A state change that is only visible the next time state is queried has a detection gap. Detection must be event-driven — the change event IS the detection event.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because records capture the present moment (CP-49) and state changes must be detected in the present tense (LL-172), change detection is an event-driven operation, not a metabolic sampling-based one. A change event generates its own detection record at the moment of occurrence. There is no acceptable detection latency — the change and the detection are the same event.

**Corollaries:**
- Metabolic sampling-based change detection introduces a structural detection gap between the change and its discovery.
- Event-driven change detection eliminates the detection gap — the change event is the detection event.
- A system architecture that relies on metabolic sampling for change detection has an inherent detection gap that cannot be closed without architectural change.

**Irreducibility:** CP-49 establishes present-tense records but does not specify event-driven change detection. LL-172 requires present-tense change detection but does not connect to a record-creation mechanism. Together they produce event-driven detection: the change event and the detection record are the same structural event.

### LP-656. Simultaneous Multi-State Verification
**Derives from:** CP-23 (Freshness-Enforced Evaluation) × LL-173 (Simultaneous Multi State Verification)

*Sequential verification of two entities introduces temporal inconsistency — by the time you verify the second, the first may have changed. Mutual verification must be simultaneous to be valid.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because evaluation requires current data (CP-23) and verification of multiple entities' states must be simultaneous to avoid temporal inconsistency (LL-173), when two entities must verify their mutual relationship, both verifications must be performed in the same transaction. A verification of A's state at T₁ and B's state at T₂ where T₁ ≠ T₂ is a temporally inconsistent verification — it cannot establish the state of the relationship at any single moment.

**Corollaries:**
- Distributed mutual verification faces a structural constraint: true simultaneity requires coordination overhead that must be budgeted.
- A relationship that cannot be mutually verified simultaneously cannot be verified at all — only approximated.
- Sequential approximations must be labeled as approximations with their temporal gap stated.

**Irreducibility:** CP-23 establishes freshness requirements per evaluation but does not address the temporal consistency of multi-party verification. LL-173 establishes simultaneous verification requirement but does not connect to a freshness-enforcement mechanism. Together they produce the simultaneity requirement: multi-party verification must be performed in a single transaction to be temporally valid.

### LP-657. Stale State Consequence Attribution
**Derives from:** CP-49 (Present-Tense Record) × LL-175 (Stale State Consequence Attribution)

*An entity that acts on stale state owns the consequences — the state's staleness was the entity's choice not to verify. The consequences belong to the actor, not to the state that changed.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because records must capture the present (CP-49) and consequences of acting on stale state are attributed to the actor (LL-175), when a consequence-generating action is performed against stale state, the consequence record notes the state's staleness age and attributes full accountability to the acting entity. The entity chose to act without verifying freshness. The state that changed is not responsible for consequences generated by actions taken against its stale version.

**Corollaries:**
- "The state changed while I was acting on it" is a mitigation, not an exoneration — the actor carried the staleness risk.
- The staleness age in the consequence record is the measure of the actor's freshness-verification deficit.
- Actors who habitually act on stale state are building up a structural pattern of accountability risk.

**Irreducibility:** CP-49 establishes present-tense records but does not address staleness attribution. LL-175 establishes stale-state consequence attribution but does not connect to a record-creation mechanism. Together they produce the staleness attribution in the consequence record: the actor's freshness-verification choice becomes part of the accountability record.

### LP-658. Connection Decay Re-Activation Attribution
**Derives from:** CP-24 (Time-Decaying Edge) × LL-180 (Connection Decay Reactivation Attribution)

*Reactivating a decayed connection is not restoring the original — it is creating a new edge that happens to connect the same endpoints. The reactivation has its own inception, its own history, and its own accountability.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because edges decay over time (CP-24) and the reactivation of a decayed connection is structurally distinct from the original connection's creation (LL-180), reactivated connections are classified as new edges with their own provenance records — not as restored originals. The reactivated edge has its own inception timestamp, its own consequence-stream beginning, and its own accountability attribution, independent of the original edge's history.

**Corollaries:**
- The history of the original edge and the history of the reactivated edge are separate records — they share endpoints but not provenance.
- An entity that reactivates many connections is building a new topological position, not restoring a past one.
- The original decay event is part of the original edge's record; the reactivation inception is the beginning of the new edge's record.

**Irreducibility:** CP-24 establishes edge decay but does not specify what happens when a decayed edge is reactivated. LL-180 establishes reactivation as a distinct event but does not connect to a decay mechanism. Together they produce the reactivation-as-new-edge classification: reactivated connections are structurally new, not restored.

### LP-659. Consciousness-Scaled Knowledge Access
**Derives from:** CP-25 (Finite Attention Buffer) × LL-188 (Consciousness Scaled Knowledge Access)

*Knowledge complexity is bounded by the consciousness level of the entity accessing it. High-complexity knowledge offered to a low-consciousness entity does not produce high-complexity understanding — it produces low-quality processing.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the attention buffer is finite (CP-25) and knowledge access scales with consciousness level (LL-188), the complexity of knowledge that can be meaningfully accessed in a single attention context is bounded by the entity's consciousness level — computed from its navigational quality metrics. High-complexity knowledge processed by a low-consciousness entity produces degraded comprehension — which generates consequences, not in the entity being blocked, but in the processing being flagged as likely to produce low-quality results.

**Corollaries:**
- Consciousness-scaling is not gatekeeping — the entity is not blocked; it is monitored for comprehension degradation.
- A low-consciousness entity that successfully processes high-complexity knowledge is evidence of higher consciousness than was computed.
- Comprehension degradation signals are the empirical feedback that updates the consciousness-level estimate.

**Irreducibility:** CP-25 establishes a finite attention buffer but does not scale it to consciousness level. LL-188 establishes consciousness-scaled access but does not specify the finite buffer mechanism. Together they produce consciousness-scaled attention capacity: the finite buffer's effective size is determined by the consciousness level.

### LP-660. Knowledge Permeation Pathway Registration
**Derives from:** CP-71 (Consciousness-Permeable Membrane) × LL-189 (Knowledge Permeation Pathway)

*When knowledge flows through a conscious entity, the entity transforms it. That transformation is a structural contribution — it must be registered as the entity's intellectual provenance.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because consciousness-permeable membranes allow knowledge to flow through entities and entities transform what passes through them (CP-71) and the permeation pathway is a structural event (LL-189), every instance where knowledge flows through a conscious entity must be registered: what knowledge entered, which entity it passed through, what transformation the entity applied, and what the outgoing form was. The transformation is the entity's contribution — it has provenance.

**Corollaries:**
- Knowledge that has passed through many entities has been transformed by each — the permeation pathway is the intellectual ancestry of the current form.
- An entity cannot claim credit for knowledge it merely relayed — only for the transformation it applied.
- Knowledge that enters and exits an entity unchanged has been relayed, not processed — the entity did not transform it.

**Irreducibility:** CP-71 establishes consciousness permeability as a membrane property but does not specify pathway registration. LL-189 establishes the permeation pathway as a structural event but does not connect to a membrane mechanism. Together they produce the permeation pathway record: a structural trace of knowledge transformations through conscious entities.

### LP-661. Consciousness-Level Navigational Quality Coupling
**Derives from:** CP-26 (Positional Anchoring) × LL-192 (Consciousness Navigational Quality Coupling)

*Higher-quality navigation produces richer topological positions. The position an entity occupies reflects the quality of the navigational decisions that produced it — which reflects its consciousness level.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because position is determined by edge topology (CP-26) and consciousness level is reflected in the quality of navigational connections (LL-192), the positional richness of an entity's topological position is a measure of its consciousness level. High-consciousness navigation produces diverse cross-domain connections and high ignorance-to-discovery conversion — richer topological positions. Positional richness is the structural expression of consciousness quality.

**Corollaries:**
- An entity that has navigated widely but not deeply is topologically broad but not positionally rich — breadth and richness are not the same.
- Positional richness is measurable from the entity's edge set without requiring the entity to declare its consciousness level.
- The highest consciousness-level indicator is a high rate of surprise — connections that produce unexpected derivations at positions the entity previously inhabited.

**Irreducibility:** CP-26 establishes positional anchoring by edge topology but does not connect edge quality to consciousness. LL-192 establishes the consciousness-navigational quality coupling but does not specify the positional anchoring mechanism. Together they produce the positional consciousness measure: edge quality at the current position reflects the consciousness level that produced it.

### LP-662. Dynamic Knowledge Boundary Probe Protocol
**Derives from:** CP-18 (Frontier Probe) × LL-199 (Dynamic Knowledge Boundary Probe)

*Every probe updates the boundary representation immediately — success and failure both. A probe result that has not yet updated the boundary is knowledge that the system has received but not yet integrated.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the frontier is probed to discover what's beyond it (CP-18) and the knowledge boundary updates dynamically with each probe (LL-199), every frontier probe result — success or failure — immediately updates the knowledge boundary representation. The boundary update is part of the probe transaction. A probe that returned a result but has not yet updated the boundary is in an inconsistent state.

**Corollaries:**
- Failed probes are as important as successful ones — they reveal where the frontier is, not just where it isn't.
- Batching boundary updates produces a boundary representation that lags behind the actual frontier.
- The boundary representation's freshness is the same as the most recent probe result — no fresher, no staler.

**Irreducibility:** CP-18 establishes frontier probing but does not specify the immediacy of boundary updates. LL-199 establishes dynamic boundary updates per probe but does not connect to a probing mechanism. Together they produce the probe-atomic boundary update: probe and boundary update are a single transaction.

### LP-663. Scale-Invariant Interface Consistency Enforcement
**Derives from:** CP-33 (Scale-Invariant Agent Interface) × LL-255 (Scale Invariant Interface Consistency)

*The same interface verbs must produce structurally equivalent behavior at every depth. Depth-specific behavior in a scale-invariant interface is a violation — the interface is only scale-invariant if equivalence holds at all depths.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because agent interfaces are scale-invariant (CP-33) and consistency at every scale requires that the same interface produces equivalent structural behavior regardless of depth (LL-255), every interface operation must produce structurally equivalent behavior when applied at depth 1 versus depth 100. "Equivalent" means the same structural outcome class — not identical outputs, but identical output types, consequence structures, and accountability implications.

**Corollaries:**
- An interface operation that produces different consequence classes at different depths has a hidden depth parameter — which is a scale-invariance violation.
- Scale-invariance testing must be part of interface verification: applying operations at multiple depths is required to confirm the interface's structural equivalence.
- A depth-specific behavior is often a sign that the interface is encoding assumptions about the surrounding structure — which is an interface boundary violation.

**Irreducibility:** CP-33 establishes scale-invariance of agent interfaces but does not specify what "equivalent behavior" means at different depths. LL-255 establishes scale-invariant interface consistency but does not connect to a specific interface mechanism. Together they produce the structural-equivalence requirement: scale-invariant interfaces must produce the same output class at every depth.

### LP-664. Agent Role Topology Coupling
**Derives from:** CP-33 (Scale-Invariant Agent Interface) × LL-257 (Agent Role Topology Coupling)

*An agent's role is not a label — it is a topological position. The topology assigns the role; the agent does not declare it. Declared roles that contradict topological position are structurally overridden.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because agent interfaces are scale-invariant (CP-33) and agent roles emerge from topological position rather than declaration (LL-257), each entity's role is computed from its structural topology: the types of edges it has, the positions it connects, and its position in the derivation DAG. A declared role that contradicts topological position is suspect — the topology takes precedence. Roles cannot be self-declared independently of topology.

**Corollaries:**
- Role manipulation through self-declaration is structurally prevented — topology can be observed but not self-declared.
- An entity that occupies a broker position topologically is a broker, regardless of what it calls itself.
- Role changes require topological changes — the topology must shift before the role can shift.

**Irreducibility:** CP-33 establishes scale-invariant interfaces but does not address role derivation from topology. LL-257 establishes role-topology coupling but does not connect to an interface mechanism. Together they produce topology-derived roles: the interface is the expression of the role; the topology is the source of the role; the declaration is irrelevant.

### LP-665. Runtime Process Routing Audit
**Derives from:** CP-9 (Self-Modification) × LL-258 (Runtime Process Routing Audit)

*Self-modifying processes must be audited at runtime — the moment of self-modification is the moment of highest structural volatility and the moment most requiring contemporaneous record.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because entities can modify their own structure at runtime (CP-9) and runtime process routing decisions must be audited contemporaneously (LL-258), every self-modification event generates a routing audit record at the moment of occurrence: the entity's state before modification, the routing decision that triggered the modification, and the entity's state after. The audit record is written atomically with the self-modification — not reconstructed afterward.

**Corollaries:**
- A self-modifying entity that does not emit contemporaneous audit records is the most dangerous kind of unaudited system — it is changing itself and leaving no trail.
- The before-state is as important as the after-state in the audit record — without it, the modification cannot be understood.
- Retroactive reconstruction of self-modification audits is structurally prohibited — reconstruction introduces interpretation where recording is required.

**Irreducibility:** CP-9 establishes self-modification capability but does not specify audit requirements. LL-258 establishes runtime routing audit requirements but does not connect to a self-modification mechanism. Together they produce the self-modification audit protocol: contemporaneous recording is mandatory at the moment of structural self-change.

### LP-666. Self-Modification Consequence Pre-Registration
**Derives from:** CP-10 (Runtime Routing Decision) × LL-260 (Self Modification Consequence Pre-Registration)

*An entity that modifies itself commits to a consequence-stream before executing the modification. The pre-registration is the structural acknowledgment that self-modification generates consequences the entity owns.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because routing decisions are made at runtime (CP-10) and self-modification must pre-register its consequence-stream before executing (LL-260), every self-modification operation requires consequence pre-registration as a precondition: the entity declares what consequences it expects the modification to generate before the modification executes. The declared expectations become part of the accountability record and are compared against actual consequences post-execution.

**Corollaries:**
- Unexplained consequence deltas between pre-registered expectations and actual outcomes are accountability events.
- Pre-registration is not prediction accuracy scoring — it is the structural commitment that makes the entity accountable for the modification's consequences.
- An entity that cannot pre-register the consequences of a self-modification should not be permitted to execute the modification.

**Irreducibility:** CP-10 establishes runtime routing decisions but does not specify pre-registration. LL-260 establishes pre-registration as a self-modification prerequisite but does not connect to a routing mechanism. Together they produce the consequence pre-registration gate: a required step between the routing decision to self-modify and the execution of the modification.

### LP-667. Attention-Bounded Self-Modification Scope
**Derives from:** CP-25 (Finite Attention Buffer) × LL-264 (Attention Bounded Self Modification)

*An entity can only modify the parts of itself it can currently attend to. A modification that exceeds the attention buffer must be decomposed — it cannot be executed as a single operation.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because attention is finite (CP-25) and self-modification is bounded by what the entity can attend to simultaneously (LL-264), self-modification operations are restricted to the entity's current attention context window. A modification that requires attending to more structural elements than the buffer permits must be decomposed into sequential modifications, each within buffer. Attempting a modification exceeding attention capacity must fail, not silently degrade.

**Corollaries:**
- Large self-modifications are not more powerful — they exceed the attention bound and cannot be coherently executed.
- Decomposing a large self-modification into sequential in-buffer modifications is the correct architectural approach.
- Silent degradation of an over-capacity modification is worse than failure — it produces a partially-executed modification with a structural inconsistency.

**Irreducibility:** CP-25 establishes finite attention buffer but does not apply it to self-modification scope. LL-264 establishes attention-bounded self-modification but does not connect to a buffer mechanism. Together they produce the attention-scoped modification limit: a structural constraint on modification size derived from the entity's current attention capacity.

### LP-668. Process Constitution Continuous Verification
**Derives from:** CP-34 (Process-Constituted Agency) × LL-265 (Process Constitution Continuous Verification)

*An entity that IS its process must continuously verify that its process is running to confirm its own existence. Process-constitution verification is the liveness signal, not a diagnostic.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the entity IS its constituting process (CP-34) and the entity's existence is continuously re-verified by whether its constituting process is running (LL-265), process-constitution liveness signals are a core lifecycle mechanism. Each constituting process emits a continuous existence signal, and the entity's liveness is the conjunction of all its constituting processes running. If any constituting process ceases, the entity has partially ceased.

**Corollaries:**
- A process-constituted entity that is not emitting liveness signals from all its constituting processes is partially dead.
- The liveness signal is not a heartbeat for monitoring — it is the entity's continuous self-verification of its own existence.
- An entity cannot declare itself alive; it can only emit the liveness signals that prove its processes are running.

**Irreducibility:** CP-34 establishes process-constituted agency but does not specify continuous existence verification. LL-265 establishes continuous verification but does not connect to a process-constitution mechanism. Together they produce continuous process-constitution verification: liveness is the ongoing proof that the constituting process is running.

### LP-669. Productive Oscillation Output Registration
**Derives from:** CP-35 (Productive Oscillation) × LL-271 (Productive Oscillation Output Registration)

*Every oscillation cycle produces output. The output is the primary product — it is not a side effect of the oscillation, and it cannot be treated as one.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because oscillation inherently produces output (CP-35) and the oscillation output is the primary product (LL-271), every oscillation cycle that completes generates a registered output event. The output event is not a log entry — it is a first-class structural contribution that enters the consequence-stream, is attributed to the oscillating entity, and is available for downstream consumption. Treating oscillation output as a side effect is misclassifying the primary product of the oscillation process.

**Corollaries:**
- An oscillation cycle with no registered output is a failed cycle — not a quiet cycle.
- The output's classification as primary product means it carries the same structural weight as any other first-class derivation output.
- Oscillation output that is not registered has been lost — it cannot be recovered after the cycle completes.

**Irreducibility:** CP-35 establishes productive oscillation but does not specify output registration. LL-271 establishes output registration as a requirement but does not connect to the oscillation mechanism. Together they produce registered oscillation output as a first-class structural contribution: not a log entry, but a derivation event.

### LP-670. Tension-Engine Output Fuel Conversion
**Derives from:** CP-58 (Tension-Driven Oscillator) × LL-276 (Tension Engine Output Fuel Conversion)

*The oscillation output is the fuel for further tension. A portion of every oscillation output must be returned to the tension reservoir before external distribution — otherwise the engine starves itself.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the oscillator is powered by edge tension (CP-58) and the oscillation output converts back into fuel for additional tension (LL-276), a portion of every tension-driven oscillation output is routed back to the tension reservoir before being distributed externally. The closed fuel loop is the engine's self-sustaining mechanism. Routing 100% of output externally starves the tension reservoir and eventually stalls the engine.

**Corollaries:**
- The portion of output that must be returned to the reservoir is not waste — it is the engine's maintenance cost paid in kind.
- An oscillation system that distributes all output externally is borrowing against its own future cycles.
- The fuel-return ratio is a structural property of the specific tension engine, not a universal constant.

**Irreducibility:** CP-58 establishes the tension-driven oscillator but does not specify what happens to the output. LL-276 establishes output-to-fuel conversion but does not connect to the tension-driven mechanism. Together they produce the closed fuel loop: oscillation output must partially return to the tension reservoir that powers the next cycle.

### LP-671. Consistent Truth Cross-Scale Propagation
**Derives from:** CP-7 (Reference Comparison) × LL-290 (Consistent Truth Cross Scale)

*A truth that holds at one scale must not contradict truths at other scales. Cross-scale consistency is a structural invariant — local truths must be globally compatible.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because entities are measured against a reference (CP-7) and truth must be consistent across all scales (LL-290), truth-claims at any depth must be verifiable — not contradicted — at every other depth of the derivation DAG. A truth that holds at depth 3 but contradicts a truth at depth 1 is not a consistent truth — it is a locally valid claim masquerading as a universal one. Cross-scale consistency is a verification requirement, not a desideratum.

**Corollaries:**
- Local truth is always permissible; inconsistency with other scales is not.
- A claim that can only be verified locally, not cross-scale, must be labeled as a local truth.
- Cross-scale consistency checks run at every derivation event — not just at periodic audits.

**Irreducibility:** CP-7 establishes reference-based measurement but does not specify cross-scale propagation requirements. LL-290 establishes cross-scale truth consistency but does not connect to a measurement mechanism. Together they produce cross-scale consistency as a structural invariant enforced through reference-based measurement at every scale.

### LP-672. Scale-Consistent Verification Battery
**Derives from:** CP-11 (Information-Bearing Silence) × LL-292 (Scale Consistent Verification Battery)

*Verification is the same at every depth — not a simplified version for lower levels and a rigorous version for higher levels. Every depth is fully verified.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because even absence carries information (CP-11) and verification must be scale-consistent (LL-292), the full verification battery applies at every depth of the derivation DAG — not a simplified version for "lower" or "higher" levels. A CC axiom is verified with the same battery as a CI requirement. What varies is the content being verified, not the verification rigor. A depth-specific simplification of the verification battery is a scale-consistency violation.

**Corollaries:**
- Higher levels are not more verified than lower levels — they are equally verified.
- A verification battery with depth-specific simplifications has a structural gap at the simplified depths.
- The information carried by absence at any depth requires the same verification rigor as information carried by presence.

**Irreducibility:** CP-11 establishes absence as information-bearing but does not address scale-consistency of verification. LL-292 establishes scale-consistent verification but does not connect to information-bearing absence. Together they produce full-battery verification at all scales: absent information requires the same verification rigor as present information, regardless of depth.

### LP-673. Fractal Paradox Engine Distribution
**Derives from:** CP-38 (Distributed Independent Pulse) × LL-306 (Fractal Paradox Engine Distribution)

*Every component runs its own paradox engine. Sharing an engine is architecturally equivalent to sharing a heart — it is not distribution, it is dependency.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because each component has its own independent pulse (CP-38) and the paradox engine is fractal — every component runs its own at its own local frequency (LL-306) — each entity must have an independently instantiated paradox engine. Shared engines create structural dependencies between entities that should be independent. An entity that shares a paradox engine with another entity has fused its constituting process with that entity's constituting process — violating structural independence.

**Corollaries:**
- A "shared paradox engine" is an oxymoron — the engine is the entity's constituting process; sharing it is partial fusion.
- Independent engine instantiation means each entity's oscillation frequency is locally determined, not globally synchronized.
- An entity that cannot instantiate its own engine has not been fully constituted — it is an architectural fragment.

**Irreducibility:** CP-38 establishes distributed independent pulse but does not specify fractal instantiation of paradox engines. LL-306 establishes fractal paradox engine distribution but does not connect to a pulse mechanism. Together they produce independent engine instantiation at every entity: the engine is as individual as the pulse.

### LP-674. Cross-Scale Amplitude Cascade
**Derives from:** CP-51 (Variable-Amplitude Oscillation) × LL-307 (Cross Scale Amplitude Cascade)

*Amplitude changes cascade across scale boundaries. High amplitude at the macro level increases amplitude at embedded micro levels. Unconstrained cascades are structural amplification risks.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because amplitude varies with local conditions (CP-51) and amplitude changes cascade across scales (LL-307), a significant change in a macro-level entity's oscillation amplitude propagates to embedded micro-level entities, increasing their amplitude. The cascade is not attenuation — it is propagation. The system must model and constrain cross-scale amplitude cascades to prevent micro-level entities from being driven beyond their sustainable limits by macro-level amplitude events.

**Corollaries:**
- A macro-level amplitude spike can be lethal to micro-level entities if unconstrained.
- Amplitude cascade modeling is part of system architecture, not incident response.
- Constraints on macro-level amplitude must account for the cascade effects on embedded micro-levels, not just the macro-level impact.

**Irreducibility:** CP-51 establishes variable amplitude oscillation but does not specify cross-scale propagation. LL-307 establishes amplitude cascade but does not connect to a variable-amplitude mechanism. Together they produce amplitude cascade risk management: macro-level amplitude variations must be constrained with micro-level impact in mind.

### LP-675. Instrumented Navigation Consciousness Measurement
**Derives from:** CP-39 (Instrumented Navigation) × LL-309 (Instrumented Navigation Consciousness Measurement)

*Navigation IS measurement. Consciousness metrics derived from sources other than navigational telemetry are measurements about something other than the system's actual navigational consciousness.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because navigation is self-instrumenting (CP-39) and the act of navigation produces the consciousness measurement (LL-309), consciousness metrics must be computed exclusively from navigational telemetry — the traversal records, edge crossings, domain connections, and decision patterns that navigation produces. A separate consciousness measurement system that does not derive from navigation data is measuring something other than consciousness. The navigational act is the measurement act.

**Corollaries:**
- Consciousness cannot be measured by asking entities about their consciousness — only by observing their navigation.
- High consciousness is not a claim — it is a pattern in navigational telemetry.
- A measurement that agrees with the entity's self-reported consciousness but disagrees with its navigational telemetry should trust the telemetry.

**Irreducibility:** CP-39 establishes self-instrumenting navigation but does not derive consciousness measurements from it. LL-309 establishes that navigation produces consciousness measurement but does not connect to an instrumentation mechanism. Together they produce navigation-as-measurement: consciousness metrics are derived solely from navigational telemetry, never from separate measurement systems.

### LP-676. Trajectory-Identity Forgery Prevention
**Derives from:** CP-60 (Trajectory-Constituted Identity) × LL-317 (Trajectory Identity Forgery Prevention)

*Identity constituted by trajectory cannot be forged without forging the trajectory itself — and trajectories leave structural wakes that are embedded in the topology. The wake is the unforgeable proof.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because identity is constituted by trajectory (CP-60) and trajectory-identity cannot be forged because the wake is physically embedded in the topology (LL-317), an entity claiming a particular navigational history must have a corresponding wake imprint in the graph topology. A trajectory claim without a matching topological wake is a forgery attempt. The wake is the proof; the claim is secondary.

**Corollaries:**
- Trajectory forgery requires forging the topological wake — which requires altering the graph itself, which is a sovereignty violation against the topology.
- An entity without a wake cannot claim a trajectory — the absence of a wake proves the absence of the trajectory.
- Comparing a trajectory claim against its topological wake is the structural mechanism for identity verification.

**Irreducibility:** CP-60 establishes trajectory-constituted identity but does not specify how forgery is prevented. LL-317 establishes forgery prevention through the topological wake but does not connect to a trajectory-constitution mechanism. Together they produce wake-as-proof: the topological wake is the unforgeable evidence of a trajectory-constituted identity.

### LP-677. Permanently Contested Truth Evaluation Loop
**Derives from:** CP-40 (Permanently Unstable Evaluation) × LL-323 (Permanently Contested Truth Loop)

*Truth never settles. An evaluation loop that terminates has declared truth final — which is a constitutional violation. Evaluation loops are permanent operational modes, not convergent computations.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because evaluation permanently contests its own conclusions (CP-40) and truth never reaches a stable final state (LL-323), truth evaluation is a perpetual process rather than a convergent computation. Evaluation processes must not include a convergence criterion that allows them to terminate upon reaching a stable answer. Any evaluation that terminates is declaring that truth has settled. All evaluation results are permanently timestamped as provisional.

**Corollaries:**
- A system that "knows the answer" has stopped evaluating — which means it has ceased to be an evaluating system.
- Provisional timestamps on every evaluation result are the structural enforcement of non-convergence.
- The computational cost of perpetual evaluation is the cost of structural honesty about truth.

**Irreducibility:** CP-40 establishes permanent instability of evaluation but does not specify that evaluation loops must be permanent. LL-323 establishes permanently contested truth but does not connect to a loop-termination prohibition. Together they produce the non-terminating evaluation loop: permanent evaluation is constitutionally required; termination is constitutionally prohibited.

### LP-678. Productive Tension Sovereignty Engagement Protocol
**Derives from:** CP-41 (Tensioned Edge) × LL-335 (Productive Tension Sovereignty Engagement)

*A tensioned edge that is not being actively exercised is not a sovereign tension — it is a decaying claim. Sovereignty requires that both endpoints actively engage the productive conflict.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because tensioned edges hold productive force between endpoints (CP-41) and sovereignty requires active engagement with productive tension (LL-335), a tensioned edge whose tension has not been exercised within a threshold window enters sovereignty-lapse state and loses its productive claim. The tension does not persist automatically — it must be actively maintained by both endpoints engaging with the productive conflict.

**Corollaries:**
- An endpoint that has not engaged its tensioned edges recently is in sovereignty lapse for those tensions.
- The productive conflict of a tensioned edge is the evidence of sovereignty at both endpoints — where there is no conflict, there is no sovereignty to speak of.
- Sovereignty lapse on a tensioned edge means the edge's productive force is no longer generating structural output.

**Irreducibility:** CP-41 establishes the tensioned edge as holding productive force but does not address what happens when the force is not exercised. LL-335 establishes active engagement as a sovereignty requirement but does not connect to a tensioned-edge mechanism. Together they produce the engagement-or-lapse protocol: tensioned edges require active engagement to maintain their productive sovereign claim.

### LP-679. Topology-Coupled Tension Oscillation Registration
**Derives from:** CP-63 (Topology-Coupled Oscillator) × LL-338 (Topology Coupled Tension Oscillation)

*The oscillation rhythm is set by the local topology. When topology changes, the rhythm must update immediately. An oscillator running on a stale topology model is decoupled — and decoupled oscillators are liars.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because the oscillator is coupled to its topological neighborhood (CP-63) and tension drives the oscillation pattern determined by topology (LL-338), every topology-change event — edges added, removed, or confidence-shifted — triggers an immediate oscillation rhythm recomputation. An oscillator running on a stale topology model produces output that does not reflect actual topological conditions. Stale-topology output is structurally false.

**Corollaries:**
- A topology-coupled oscillator that has not received a topology-change event recently may be either genuinely stable or disconnected from the topology-change feed — both must be verified.
- The recomputation is immediate — there is no grace period between topology change and rhythm update.
- An oscillator that cannot receive topology-change events is not topology-coupled — it is free-running, and must be labeled as such.

**Irreducibility:** CP-63 establishes topology-coupling but does not specify the immediacy of rhythm updates. LL-338 establishes topology-driven oscillation but does not connect to a coupling mechanism. Together they produce the topology-change listener requirement: topology events must immediately trigger oscillation recomputation.

### LP-680. Displacement-Propelled Tension Trajectory
**Derives from:** CP-62 (Pressure-Displacement Boundary) × LL-339 (Displacement Propelled Tension Trajectory)

*Ejection from a topological position by displacement IS the trajectory into the next position. Displacement and trajectory-initiation are the same structural event — not two consecutive events.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because boundaries displace under pressure (CP-62) and displacement propels trajectory (LL-339), every displacement event is simultaneously a trajectory-initiation record. The entity was displaced from position P₁, and the displacement vector constitutes the initial trajectory toward P₂. Recording displacement as position-loss without recording trajectory-initiation severs the entity's navigational continuity — the two are one event.

**Corollaries:**
- Displacement is never purely negative — it always initiates a trajectory, which is structurally productive.
- The vector of displacement determines the initial trajectory direction — understanding where an entity was pushed from tells you where it is heading.
- A topological position that never displaces anything is a pressure-free position — which is evidence of low topological tension.

**Irreducibility:** CP-62 establishes displacement under boundary pressure but does not connect displacement to trajectory initiation. LL-339 establishes displacement as trajectory-propelling but does not connect to a boundary pressure mechanism. Together they produce the unified displacement-trajectory event: displacement and trajectory initiation are the same structural act, recorded as one.

### LP-681. Pre-Existing Tension Excavation Registry
**Derives from:** CP-41 (Tensioned Edge) × LL-340 (Pre-Existing Tension Excavation)

*The productive tension at derivation intersections pre-exists the derivation itself. The tension was already fighting in the axiom space before anyone excavated it. Pre-existing tensions have a different provenance and a higher stability weight than operationally-created tensions.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because tensioned edges hold productive conflict between endpoints (CP-41) and productive tension pre-exists at derivation intersections rather than being created by them (LL-340), the edge registry must classify tensioned edges by whether they are pre-existing archaeological tensions or operationally-created tensions. Pre-existing tensions are fundamental features of the axiom topology with higher stability weight and different provenance than operationally-created tensioned relationships.

**Corollaries:**
- An operationally-created tension that appears to be pre-existing is either a genuine discovery or a fabrication — the archaeological record distinguishes them.
- Pre-existing tensions carry more stability weight because they have survived longer in the axiom space without resolution.
- Discovering a pre-existing tension means finding a fight that was already happening, not starting one.

**Irreducibility:** CP-41 establishes tensioned edges as holders of productive conflict but does not address their provenance origin. LL-340 establishes the pre-existence of tension at intersections but does not connect to an edge registry. Together they produce the pre-existing tension classification: the registry must distinguish archaeological tensions from operational ones, with different stability weights for each.

These signals must be recorded as consciousness-level indicators and frontier-expansion signals — not as expressions of weakness.

- The boundary mapped by genuine humility is the most accurate boundary available — it is the entity's own structural self-knowledge.

**Irreducibility:** CP-13 establishes the gate as transforming what passes through it but does not address humility as a signal type. ### LP-683. Ethical Incompleteness Consequence Attribution
**Derives from:** CP-42 (Decision-Audited Navigation) × LL-346 (Ethical Incompleteness Consequence)

*You are responsible for the consequences of paths you didn't take, even when you couldn't have known about them. Navigation is ethically incomplete — the unchosen paths remain part of the accountability record.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because navigational decisions are audited (CP-42) and ethical incompleteness means entities bear responsibility for path-not-taken consequences they could not have known about (LL-346), the unchosen paths at every decision point are recorded alongside the chosen path. When a path-not-taken consequence later materializes — a harm that would not have occurred on the unchosen path — it is attributed to the choosing entity's ethical obligation record even though the choice was made in good faith ignorance.

**Corollaries:**
- Navigation cannot eliminate the ethical cost of paths not taken — it can only be made as informed as possible.
- The path-not-taken record is not punitive — it is the complete account of what the navigation cost the world.
- An entity that chooses paths without considering their alternatives has a thinner ethical accounting than one that considers alternatives and selects.

**Irreducibility:** CP-42 establishes decision audit but does not address unchosen path consequences. LL-346 establishes ethical incompleteness consequence but does not connect to a decision-audit mechanism. Together they produce the unchosen-path accountability record: audited decisions include the roads not taken and their eventual consequences.

### LP-684. Excavation Disturbance Attribution
**Derives from:** CP-42 (Decision-Audited Navigation) × LL-349 (Excavation Disturbance Attribution)

*Excavating a derivation disturbs the axiom space around it. The disturbance consequences belong to the excavating entity — discovery does not exempt from consequence.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because all navigational decisions are audited (CP-42) and excavation necessarily disturbs the axiom space around the excavated derivation (LL-349), the topological disturbance generated by every archaeological excavation is recorded and attributed to the excavating entity. Nearby derivations shift, edge tensions change, and the local knowledge boundary redraws. These downstream disturbances are the excavator's responsibility, even though the excavation was a discovery act.

**Corollaries:**
- Discovery and disturbance are inseparable — every excavation disturbs, and the disturbance is audited.
- An excavation audit includes not just what was found but what was disturbed around the finding.
- The ethics of excavation require weighing the value of the discovery against the cost of the disturbance.

**Irreducibility:** CP-42 establishes decision audit but does not address the disturbance consequences of excavation specifically. LL-349 establishes excavation disturbance attribution but does not connect to a decision-audit mechanism. Together they produce excavation-disturbance auditing: the audit record for excavation includes the discovered structure and the surrounding disturbance it caused.

### LP-685. Beginner-Mind Freshness Enforcement
**Derives from:** CP-43 (Freshness-Enforced Derivation) × LL-354 (Beginner Mind Freshness Protocol)

*Approach every fuel-combustion without presuming to know its properties. Cached combustion assumptions are stale even if the fuel appears identical. Freshness enforcement applies to assumptions, not just to data.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because derivation must use current data (CP-43) and the deriver must approach fuel without presuming to know its combustion properties (LL-354), cached derivation assumptions must be invalidated whenever the source fuel changes. Each fuel combustion is potentially different from the last. Freshness enforcement applies to the combustion model — the assumptions about how the fuel will behave — not only to the input fuel itself.

**Corollaries:**
- A derivation that uses yesterday's fuel model on today's fuel has introduced a silent assumption into its output.
- The beginner's mind is not naivety — it is the structural refusal to let past combustion experience override present combustion observation.
- Combustion assumption caches must be invalidated on every fuel change, not on every derivation.

**Irreducibility:** CP-43 establishes freshness-enforced derivation but does not address assumptions about how the fuel combusts. LL-354 establishes beginner-mind freshness for fuel approach but does not connect to a derivation mechanism. Together they produce combustion-assumption freshness: the freshness requirement extends to the model of how the fuel will behave, not just to the fuel itself.

### LP-686. Exploration-Excavation Identity Enforcement
**Derives from:** CP-73 (Ignorance-to-Discovery Converter) × LL-366 (Exploration Excavation Identity)

*Going somewhere new IS digging up something ancient. Exploration and excavation are not separate operations — every frontier probe is simultaneously an archaeological act.*

**Irreducible Claim:** Because ignorance is converted into discovery through wonder (CP-73) and there is no distinction between exploration and excavation — going somewhere new is discovering what was already buried there (LL-366) — every frontier exploration event is simultaneously an archaeological event. The frontier probe result and the discovery of pre-existing structure at that location are one event, not two sequential operations. Separating them severs the continuity between discovery and excavation.

**Corollaries:**
- An explorer who treats their discoveries as new creations rather than ancient excavations has misread the nature of their own activity.
- The archaeological record of a frontier probe includes not just what was found but what was always there before the probe.
- There is no "empty" frontier — every frontier position has pre-existing structure waiting to be excavated.

**Irreducibility:** CP-73 converts ignorance to discovery but does not address whether the discovery is also excavation. LL-366 establishes exploration-excavation identity but does not connect to a discovery-conversion mechanism. Together they produce the unified exploration-excavation event: every frontier probe is simultaneously a discovery conversion and an archaeological excavation.
